Under suitable sparsity assumptions on data, the proposed algorithm is proved to enjoy faster convergence by combining diffusion model inference with sparse recovery. As a byproduct, we obtain an optimal value for the latent space dimension. We also conduct numerical experiments on a range of datasets, including image data (handwritten digits, medical images, and climate data) and financial time series for stress testing.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a dimension reduction technique called CSDM (Compressed Sensing Diffusion Models) to accelerate the inference process in diffusion-based generative models. CSDM integrates compressed sensing with diffusion models in three stages: (i) compressing data into a lower-dimensional latent space, (ii) training a diffusion model in the latent space, and (iii) using a compressed sensing algorithm to decode the generated samples back to the original data space. The paper offers a theoretical analysis of CSDM, derives an "optimal" dimension for the latent space, and conducts numerical experiments on image datasets (MNIST, OCTMNIST, ERA5 Reanalysis) and financial time series data. The experiments demonstrate that CSDM can significantly reduce wall-clock time for inference while preserving sample fidelity, especially for sparse data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of combining compressed sensing and diffusion models to improve inference speed is novel and interesting. While existing works explore latent diffusion models and acceleration of diffusion models separately, directly coupling them through a theoretical lens and providing practical implementation details offers a new perspective. The theoretical analysis, especially the derivation of an optimal latent space dimension based on sparsity assumptions and the interplay between diffusion inference time and compressed sensing recovery complexity, is a significant contribution.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in addressing a major bottleneck in diffusion models - the high computational cost of inference. By reducing the dimensionality of the data before diffusion, the method offers a practical pathway to speed up generation, especially for resource-constrained environments or applications requiring real-time generation. The applications to medical and climate data are valuable, showcasing the potential to extend the use of diffusion models beyond traditional computer vision tasks. The financial time series application demonstrates versatility, though PCA rather than compressed sensing is employed for dimensionality reduction.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear and well-structured presentation:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow.
    *   **Strong theoretical grounding:** The theoretical analysis is rigorous and provides valuable insights into the interplay of compressed sensing and diffusion models.
    *   **Comprehensive experimental validation:**  The experiments on various datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of CSDM in reducing inference time while maintaining data fidelity.
    *   **Practical implications:**  The work offers concrete strategies for accelerating diffusion model inference, which is valuable for practical applications.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Sparsity dependence:**  The performance and theoretical guarantees of CSDM heavily rely on the sparsity assumption of the data. While the paper applies it to datasets with inherent or induced sparsity (after pre-processing for OCTMNIST and using PCA for financial time series), the general applicability might be limited for dense data.  This needs to be clearly highlighted.
    *   **Assumptions on Diffusion inference complexity:**  The per-iteration diffusion inference complexity is assumed comparable to the per-iteration recovery via FISTA. Though mentioned as a subtlety, this could be limiting depending on the hardware and implementation, potentially making the theoretical analysis less precise.
    *   **Incremental Improvement in some cases**: Some experiments, specifically, on ERA5 exhibit a 4.22% improvement. This may signal a small impact in some datasets. It must be acknowledged to address the limitations of the method.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by providing a novel and efficient approach to accelerate diffusion model inference. It can inspire further research on combining compressed sensing and diffusion models, exploring other dimension reduction techniques, and developing more efficient optimization algorithms for sparse recovery in the context of generative modeling. The applications showcased can also motivate researchers to explore the use of diffusion models in other fields where efficient data generation is crucial.

*   **Missing context and related work:**
    *   Related to assumption in (4), [Chen, Chen, Chen 2024] can be cited to provide evidence for this point. The work has been published in ICLR
    *   A discussion is necessary regarding the computation cost of FISTA relative to Diffusion methods.

*   **Room for improvement in theoretical analysis**: It would be very useful if the impact of S (data sparsity) in each of the stages (a. compressed sensing, b. diffusion) were analysed.

**Score:** 7.5

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and well-analyzed approach to accelerate diffusion model inference, a significant problem in the field. The theoretical analysis and experimental results support the effectiveness of the proposed method. However, the strong dependence on data sparsity and diffusion inference assumptions, as well as the incremental improvements in some specific instances, limits its broader applicability and impact. Therefore, while a valuable contribution, it does not reach the level of an exceptional breakthrough warranting a higher score. Finally, better incorporation of recent, state-of-the-art studies in the literature must be included, as well as a discussion of implementation and computation.

- **Classification**: stat.ML
- **Score**: 7/10

### MTQA:Matrix of Thought for Enhanced Reasoning in Complex Question Answering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03918v1)
- **Authors**: Fengxiao Tang, Yufeng Li, Zongzong Wu, Ming Zhao
- **Abstract**: Complex Question Answering (QA) is a fundamental and challenging task in NLP. While large language models (LLMs) exhibit impressive performance in QA, they suffer from significant performance degradation when facing complex and abstract QA tasks due to insufficient reasoning capabilities. Works such as Chain-of-Thought (CoT) and Tree-of-Thought (ToT) aim to enhance LLMs' reasoning abilities, but they face issues such as in-layer redundancy in tree structures and single paths in chain structures. Although some studies utilize Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) methods to assist LLMs in reasoning, the challenge of effectively utilizing large amounts of information involving multiple entities and hops remains critical. To address this, we propose the Matrix of Thought (MoT), a novel and efficient LLM thought structure. MoT explores the problem in both horizontal and vertical dimensions through the "column-cell communication" mechanism, enabling LLMs to actively engage in multi-strategy and deep-level thinking, reducing redundancy within the column cells and enhancing reasoning capabilities. Furthermore, we develop a fact-correction mechanism by constructing knowledge units from retrieved knowledge graph triples and raw text to enhance the initial knowledge for LLM reasoning and correct erroneous answers. This leads to the development of an efficient and accurate QA framework (MTQA). Experimental results show that our framework outperforms state-of-the-art methods on four widely-used datasets in terms of F1 and EM scores, with reasoning time only 14.4\% of the baseline methods, demonstrating both its efficiency and accuracy. The code for this framework is available at https://github.com/lyfiter/mtqa.
- **Summary**: Here is a concise summary and a critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Matrix of Thought Question Answering (MTQA), a novel framework that enhances the reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs) in complex question answering.  MTQA utilizes a "Matrix of Thought" (MoT) structure, exploring problem-solving in horizontal and vertical dimensions through a "column-cell communication" mechanism. This approach aims to reduce redundancy, facilitate multi-strategy thinking, and deepen the level of reasoning. Additionally, the paper constructs knowledge units from retrieved knowledge graph triples and raw text to enhance the initial knowledge for LLM reasoning and correct erroneous answers.  Experiments demonstrate MTQA's superior performance on several datasets compared to state-of-the-art methods, showing improved F1 and EM scores and reduced reasoning time.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the MoT structure itself, a matrix-based reasoning framework that combines aspects of chain-of-thought (CoT) and tree-of-thought (ToT) but aims to address their limitations.  The knowledge unit construction from KG triples and raw text is an incremental but useful enhancement of existing RAG methods. The column-cell communication mechanism contributes to exploring multiple dimensions in the solution space for a problem by guiding LLMs to proactively explore alternative perspectives. However, the individual components (RAG, CoT, ToT) are not themselves new, the innovation is in their integration and structural arrangement.

*   **Significance:** The demonstrated improvements in performance metrics (F1 and EM) on several datasets, coupled with reduced reasoning time, highlight the potential of MTQA for practical applications.  Specifically, the performance boost on multi-hop QA datasets (HotpotQA and 2WikiMultihopQA) indicates a strong ability to handle complex reasoning tasks.  Furthermore, the paper tackles the important challenge of knowledge integration for LLMs, and the proposed method represents a tangible step toward more reliable and efficient QA systems.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clearly defined problem and proposed solution.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation across multiple datasets and metrics.
    *   Detailed ablation studies to demonstrate the effectiveness of individual components.
    *   Numerical analysis provided valuable insights into hyperparameter tuning.
    *   Addressing limitations of RAG and ToT effectively.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on GPT-40-mini for evaluation could limit the generalizability to other LLMs.
    *   The effectiveness is influenced by the quality of task prompts.
    *   The reliance on hyperparameters that are tuned is a major drawback.
    *   While the paper acknowledges the limitations of RAG in this context, further research on optimizing the KG and RAG component within MoT would further boost results.
    *   Requires a lot of computational power, limiting implementation.

*   **Potential Influence:** MTQA presents a promising approach to enhancing LLM reasoning for complex QA tasks. If validated and extended by further research, it could influence the design of future LLM-based QA systems and contribute to a better understanding of knowledge integration and reasoning strategies. Its matrix structure will be explored by future models for different aspects of reasoning.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** MTQA presents a reasonably innovative approach to enhancing LLM reasoning through a well-structured framework. The results demonstrate clear improvements over existing methods, and the ablation studies offer valuable insights. However, the reliance on specific LLMs, tuned hyperparameters, and the existing state of knowledge retrieval reduces the potential for the overall score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Decoding the Poetic Language of Emotion in Korean Modern Poetry: Insights from a Human-Labeled Dataset and AI Modeling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03932v1)
- **Authors**: Iro Lim, Haein Ji, Byungjun Kim
- **Abstract**: This study introduces KPoEM (Korean Poetry Emotion Mapping) , a novel dataset for computational emotion analysis in modern Korean poetry. Despite remarkable progress in text-based emotion classification using large language models, poetry-particularly Korean poetry-remains underexplored due to its figurative language and cultural specificity. We built a multi-label emotion dataset of 7,662 entries, including 7,007 line-level entries from 483 poems and 615 work-level entries, annotated with 44 fine-grained emotion categories from five influential Korean poets. A state-of-the-art Korean language model fine-tuned on this dataset significantly outperformed previous models, achieving 0.60 F1-micro compared to 0.34 from models trained on general corpora. The KPoEM model, trained through sequential fine-tuning-first on general corpora and then on the KPoEM dataset-demonstrates not only an enhanced ability to identify temporally and culturally specific emotional expressions, but also a strong capacity to preserve the core sentiments of modern Korean poetry. This study bridges computational methods and literary analysis, presenting new possibilities for the quantitative exploration of poetic emotions through structured data that faithfully retains the emotional and cultural nuances of Korean literature.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces KPOEM, a new human-labeled dataset for computational emotion analysis specifically designed for modern Korean poetry. Recognizing the limitations of applying general-purpose NLP models to poetry, due to its figurative language and cultural nuances, the authors created a dataset of 7,662 entries from five influential Korean poets, annotated with 44 fine-grained emotion categories. They demonstrate the utility of KPOEM by fine-tuning a state-of-the-art Korean language model, which significantly outperformed models trained on general corpora. The study highlights the importance of domain-specific datasets and sequential fine-tuning strategies for emotion classification in literary texts and offers new avenues for quantitative exploration of poetic emotions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The creation of the KPOEM dataset itself is a significant contribution. Domain-specific resources, particularly for languages other than English and for nuanced domains like poetry, are valuable. The paper demonstrates an awareness of existing gaps in Korean-language emotion resources and directly addresses the lack of poetry-specific datasets. The use of a multi-label emotion schema based on the KOTE dataset is also a reasonable choice, and building off existing frameworks rather than reinventing everything is a good practice.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in bridging the gap between computational methods and literary analysis, opening new avenues for exploring poetic emotions in a quantitative manner. The improved performance of the model fine-tuned on KPOEM, compared to those trained on generic corpora, validates the dataset's value. The study highlights the importance of cultural sensitivity in emotion analysis and offers valuable methodological insights for other researchers working with figurative language or culturally specific text. It provides a foundation for future research exploring emotion in Korean literature and sets an example for creating similar resources for other languages and genres.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Dataset Construction:** The paper provides a detailed description of the dataset creation process, including poet selection, data collection from public sources, preprocessing steps, and annotation guidelines. This transparency enhances the reproducibility of the research.
    *   **Quantitative Results:** The paper presents clear and compelling quantitative results demonstrating the superior performance of the KPOEM-fine-tuned model.
    *   **Qualitative Analysis:** The inclusion of a qualitative analysis adds depth to the study, providing examples of how the different models interpret specific poems.
    *   **Methodological Rigor:** The use of a multi-annotator approach and inter-rater reliability measures strengthens the validity of the annotation process.
    *   **Clear Contribution:** The paper clearly articulates its contributions and outlines potential avenues for future research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Authorial Diversity:** The dataset focuses on only five poets, which limits its generalizability to the broader corpus of modern Korean poetry. While these poets are influential, the lack of representation from other authors (particularly female authors) is a notable limitation.
    *   **Copyright Constraints:** The copyright restrictions mentioned as a selection criterion limited the dataset’s scope, and this could also introduce bias if the authors' works differ systematically from poets with more recent deaths.
    *   **Limited Novelty in the Modeling Approach:** While the application of the KcELECTRA model to KPOEM is valuable, the modeling approach itself isn't particularly novel. More sophisticated neural architectures or incorporation of literary features directly into the model architecture could have been explored for higher impact.
    *   **Modest Performance Improvement**: While there is statistically significant improvement, the gains over a model trained on a general corpus are relatively modest. It would be helpful to know the performance gain in using all the labels or only a subset, as it is likely that some emotion categories would be far more useful than others.

*   **Potential Influence:** The study has the potential to influence the field by providing a valuable resource for researchers interested in computational literary analysis and Korean literature. It could inspire similar projects for other languages and genres and contribute to the development of more culturally sensitive NLP models. The paper's emphasis on domain-specific datasets and sequential fine-tuning strategies could also inform best practices for emotion classification in other specialized domains.

**Score:** 7.5

**Justification:**

While the paper presents a solid and well-executed study with valuable contributions, the score reflects a measured assessment that considers both its strengths and weaknesses. The creation of the KPOEM dataset and the demonstrated improvement in emotion classification performance justify a positive evaluation. However, the limitations in authorial diversity, novelty in modeling approaches, and modest performance gains prevent it from achieving a higher score. This score emphasizes that the paper makes a significant contribution, yet there are avenues for improvement that could increase its impact on the field. Further, the modelling approach (using KcELECTRA) is fairly standard, and more novel modeling techniques or adaptation of architectures would likely result in a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### SelfAug: Mitigating Catastrophic Forgetting in Retrieval-Augmented Generation via Distribution Self-Alignment
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03934v1)
- **Authors**: Yuqing Huang, Rongyang Zhang, Qimeng Wang, Chengqiang Lu, Yan Gao, Yi Wu, Yao Hu, Xuyang Zhi, Guiquan Liu, Xin Li, Hao Wang, Enhong Chen
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in large language models (LLMs) have revolutionized natural language processing through their remarkable capabilities in understanding and executing diverse tasks. While supervised fine-tuning, particularly in Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) scenarios, effectively enhances task-specific performance, it often leads to catastrophic forgetting, where models lose their previously acquired knowledge and general capabilities. Existing solutions either require access to general instruction data or face limitations in preserving the model's original distribution. To overcome these limitations, we propose SelfAug, a self-distribution alignment method that aligns input sequence logits to preserve the model's semantic distribution, thereby mitigating catastrophic forgetting and improving downstream performance. Extensive experiments demonstrate that SelfAug achieves a superior balance between downstream learning and general capability retention. Our comprehensive empirical analysis reveals a direct correlation between distribution shifts and the severity of catastrophic forgetting in RAG scenarios, highlighting how the absence of RAG capabilities in general instruction tuning leads to significant distribution shifts during fine-tuning. Our findings not only advance the understanding of catastrophic forgetting in RAG contexts but also provide a practical solution applicable across diverse fine-tuning scenarios. Our code is publicly available at https://github.com/USTC-StarTeam/SelfAug.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "SelfAug: Mitigating Catastrophic Forgetting in Retrieval-Augmented Generation via Distribution Self-Alignment":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of catastrophic forgetting in large language models (LLMs) when fine-tuned for Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) tasks.  The authors propose SelfAug, a novel self-distribution alignment method. SelfAug aligns the input sequence logits during fine-tuning, preserving the model's semantic distribution and mitigating the loss of previously acquired knowledge and general capabilities.  The method does not require access to general instruction data or involve strict parameter constraints.  Experiments demonstrate that SelfAug improves downstream performance while preserving general capabilities, achieving a better balance than existing methods. The paper also highlights a correlation between distribution shifts and the severity of catastrophic forgetting in RAG scenarios, particularly with longer reference documents.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Problem Relevance:** The paper addresses a crucial and well-recognized problem in LLMs: catastrophic forgetting during fine-tuning, especially within the increasingly important context of RAG.
*   **Novel Approach:** SelfAug presents a potentially novel and practical approach by focusing on aligning input sequence logits to maintain the model's original distribution. This is a different tactic from methods that rely on data replay, parameter constraints, or knowledge reconstruction.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The paper includes extensive experiments on various benchmarks, demonstrating SelfAug's effectiveness in balancing downstream learning and general capability retention. Ablation studies are used to investigate the influence of components and a range of parameter options.
*   **Insightful Analysis:** The paper provides an empirical analysis that reveals a direct correlation between distribution shifts and the severity of catastrophic forgetting in RAG scenarios. The observation regarding the impact of longer reference documents on this shift is insightful.
*   **Clarity:** The paper is generally well-written and clearly explains the proposed method and the experimental setup.
*  **Reproducibility**: The code is publicly available.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Novelty:** While the application of logits alignment in RAG is interesting, the underlying concept of using self-distillation or output alignment to prevent catastrophic forgetting is not entirely new. Some existing methods already use similar techniques to preserve the learned distribution or to enforce self-consistency. The novelty lies in applying it to input logits for RAG. The paper could benefit from a more thorough comparison with existing self-distillation methods.
*   **Computational Cost:** While the paper mentions reduced computational overhead compared to other methods, the specific computational costs of SelfAug (especially the memory requirements of storing and aligning logits) compared to other methods should be clearly explained.
*   **Scope of Experiments:** While the paper presents a comprehensive set of experiments it can benefit from comparing against other regularization based techniques such as experience replay or weight regularization.
*  **Base Model:** All experiments are conducted with Qwen2.5-7B.

**Significance:**

The paper has the potential to be a significant contribution to the field. The problem it addresses is important, and the proposed solution is practical. The experiments provide evidence that SelfAug is effective in mitigating catastrophic forgetting while maintaining downstream performance.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a useful approach to mitigating catastrophic forgetting in RAG by aligning input logits, supported by empirical validation. The novelty, while present, is incremental since it applies input logits to a general concept that is also used by other SOTA techniques. Also, the experiments have some limitations.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**

A score of 7 is assigned because the paper tackles a relevant issue with a practical solution. However, the concept of self-alignment for mitigating catastrophic forgetting, isn't entirely novel, therefore, the overall innovation is considered incremental. While results are compelling and validated with empirical data, this is countered with limitation in the number of experiments and lack of model base experimentation. If the paper can enhance the claims of SOTA through more varied comparisons along with thorough testing using a varied set of base models would enhance the overall score. Also, the paper could benefit from presenting thorough memory and compute costs of SelfAug.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### VoxRole: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Evaluating Speech-Based Role-Playing Agents
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03940v1)
- **Authors**: Weihao Wu, Liang Cao, Xinyu Wu, Zhiwei Lin, Rui Niu, Jingbei Li, Zhiyong Wu
- **Abstract**: Recent significant advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs) have greatly propelled the development of Role-Playing Conversational Agents (RPCAs). These systems aim to create immersive user experiences through consistent persona adoption. However, current RPCA research faces dual limitations. First, existing work predominantly focuses on the textual modality, entirely overlooking critical paralinguistic features including intonation, prosody, and rhythm in speech, which are essential for conveying character emotions and shaping vivid identities. Second, the speech-based role-playing domain suffers from a long-standing lack of standardized evaluation benchmarks. Most current spoken dialogue datasets target only fundamental capability assessments, featuring thinly sketched or ill-defined character profiles. Consequently, they fail to effectively quantify model performance on core competencies like long-term persona consistency. To address this critical gap, we introduce VoxRole, the first comprehensive benchmark specifically designed for the evaluation of speech-based RPCAs. The benchmark comprises 13335 multi-turn dialogues, totaling 65.6 hours of speech from 1228 unique characters across 261 movies. To construct this resource, we propose a novel two-stage automated pipeline that first aligns movie audio with scripts and subsequently employs an LLM to systematically build multi-dimensional profiles for each character. Leveraging VoxRole, we conduct a multi-dimensional evaluation of contemporary spoken dialogue models, revealing crucial insights into their respective strengths and limitations in maintaining persona consistency.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "VoxRole: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Evaluating Speech-Based Role-Playing Agents":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces VoxRole, a novel benchmark designed specifically for evaluating speech-based role-playing conversational agents (RPCAs). The authors address the limitations of current RPCA research, which predominantly focuses on text modality and lacks standardized evaluation benchmarks for speech. VoxRole comprises a large corpus of multi-turn dialogues extracted from movies, along with multi-dimensional character profiles generated using a two-stage automated pipeline. The pipeline aligns movie audio with scripts and then uses an LLM to build character profiles encompassing personality traits, linguistic style, interpersonal relationships, and acoustic characteristics. The paper also presents a multi-dimensional evaluation framework and analyzes the performance of contemporary spoken dialogue models using VoxRole, highlighting their strengths and weaknesses in maintaining persona consistency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary strength of the paper lies in its novelty. The creation of VoxRole fills a significant gap in the RPCA research landscape by providing a comprehensive and standardized benchmark for speech-based role-playing. The automated pipeline for extracting and annotating dialogue data from movies is also a valuable contribution, as it offers a scalable alternative to manual annotation. The multi-dimensional character profiles, encompassing personality, linguistic style, relationships, and acoustics, provide a richer context for evaluation than previous datasets.

*   **Significance:** The VoxRole benchmark has the potential to significantly advance the field of speech-based RPCAs. By providing a standardized evaluation framework, it enables researchers to compare different models objectively and track progress over time. The benchmark also facilitates the development of more contextually aware and socially intelligent dialogue agents. Furthermore, the insights gained from evaluating existing models using VoxRole can guide future research directions and improve the design of RPCA systems.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Addresses a critical gap in RPCA research.
    *   Presents a novel and scalable approach for creating character-rich spoken dialogue datasets.
    *   Offers a comprehensive evaluation framework that considers multiple dimensions of role-playing.
    *   Provides valuable insights into the capabilities and limitations of contemporary spoken dialogue models.
    *   Uses the LLM-generated persona information as role-playing prompts which could potentially streamline the evaluation process.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The dataset relies on movie scripts, which may not always reflect natural human conversations. There might be inherent biases or constraints introduced by the scriptwriters.
    *   The evaluation relies partly on LLM-based judgment, which itself may be subject to biases or inconsistencies.
    *   The study might be limited by the specific set of models evaluated.  A wider range of models could provide a more robust analysis.
    *   Lack of a human-in-the-loop component in the data creation process, which could have provided higher quality dialogue or persona information, though that would have made the dataset significantly smaller.

*   **Justification for Score:**

    The paper presents a significant contribution to the field of speech-based RPCAs by providing a novel and comprehensive benchmark for evaluating model performance. While there are some limitations related to the dataset creation process and evaluation methodology, the benefits of VoxRole in advancing research and enabling objective model comparison outweigh these drawbacks. The paper is well-written, technically sound, and clearly articulates the motivation, methodology, and results of the study. The paper establishes a valuable resource that will likely be adopted by the RPCA research community.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### ANTS: Shaping the Adaptive Negative Textual Space by MLLM for OOD Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03951v1)
- **Authors**: Zhu Wenjie, Zhang Yabin, Xin Jin, Wenjun Zeng, Lei Zhang
- **Abstract**: The introduction of negative labels (NLs) has proven effective in enhancing Out-of-Distribution (OOD) detection. However, existing methods often lack an understanding of OOD images, making it difficult to construct an accurate negative space. In addition, the presence of false negative labels significantly degrades their near-OOD performance. To address these issues, we propose shaping an Adaptive Negative Textual Space (ANTS) by leveraging the understanding and reasoning capabilities of multimodal large language models (MLLMs). Specifically, we identify images likely to be OOD samples as negative images and prompt the MLLM to describe these images, generating expressive negative sentences that precisely characterize the OOD distribution and enhance far-OOD detection. For the near-OOD setting, where OOD samples resemble the in-distribution (ID) subset, we first identify the subset of ID classes that are visually similar to negative images and then leverage the reasoning capability of MLLMs to generate visually similar negative labels tailored to this subset, effectively reducing false negatives and improving near-OOD detection. To balance these two types of negative textual spaces, we design an adaptive weighted score that enables the method to handle different OOD task settings (near-OOD and far-OOD) without relying on task-specific prior knowledge, making it highly adaptable in open environments. On the ImageNet benchmark, our ANTS significantly reduces the FPR95 by 4.2\%, establishing a new state-of-the-art. Furthermore, our method is training-free and zero-shot, enabling high scalability.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ANTS (Adaptive Negative Textual Space), a novel method for Out-of-Distribution (OOD) detection that leverages multimodal large language models (MLLMs).  ANTS addresses limitations in existing negative label (NL) based OOD detection techniques, namely, a lack of understanding of OOD images, poor performance in near-OOD scenarios, and reliance on task-specific prior knowledge.  ANTS uses MLLMs to generate expressive negative sentences (ENS) describing OOD images to improve far-OOD detection. For near-OOD detection, ANTS identifies ID classes visually similar to OOD samples and generates visually similar negative labels (VSNL) tailored to those classes. Finally, it uses an adaptive weighted score to balance the ENS and VSNL spaces, enabling the method to adapt to both near-OOD and far-OOD tasks without task-specific tuning.  The paper demonstrates significant improvements in OOD detection performance on ImageNet and OpenOOD benchmarks, achieving state-of-the-art results, while being training-free and zero-shot.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper exhibits reasonable novelty.  While using MLLMs for OOD detection isn't entirely new, ANTS innovatively combines this with carefully designed prompts to create both expressive negative sentences and visually similar negative labels. The adaptive weighting mechanism is also a valuable contribution.  The key lies in the *specific* way MLLMs are leveraged and combined. Prior work has used MLLMs for OOD, but generally not with this detailed and dynamically adaptive generation of negative samples and corresponding weighting. The explicit differentiation of "far OOD" and "near OOD" and targeted generation based on this distinction is a strong point.
* **Significance:** The paper makes a significant contribution to the field of OOD detection. By addressing the limitations of existing NL-based methods, ANTS achieves substantial performance improvements, particularly regarding the challenging near-OOD detection scenario. The training-free and zero-shot nature of ANTS makes it highly scalable and practical for real-world applications. The reduction in FPR95 on ImageNet is compelling. The analysis of existing NL limitations is well articulated. Furthermore, the paper's approach enhances the explainability and interpretability of OOD detection by leveraging textual descriptions, which can be valuable for understanding why a particular sample is considered OOD. The study is extensive, with ablation experiments validating the individual components and analyzing their effects.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Strong performance gains:** The paper demonstrates significant improvements over existing methods across various benchmarks.
    * **Zero-shot and training-free:** ANTS offers excellent scalability by being training-free.
    * **Adaptive and generalizable:** The adaptive weighting mechanism allows the method to handle different OOD task settings without relying on prior knowledge.
    * **Well-designed experiments and ablations:** The experimental setup is comprehensive and includes thorough ablations to evaluate the contribution of each component.
    * **Clear articulation of limitations of prior work.**
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on a Basic OOD Detector:** The method requires a initial "basic" OOD detector (NegLabel) to select negative samples for MLLM prompting. This could be a potential dependency and might affect performance if the initial detector is not sufficiently accurate.  The paper notes robustness to ID noise but this initial dependence is a limitation.
    * **Computational Cost:** The reliance on MLLMs introduces a computational overhead, especially during inference. Though the paper claims to mitigate the impact of MLLMs, it acknowledges that test-time cost is incurred by MLLMs. Efficiency might be a barrier to deployment in real-time and resource-constrained applications. Future work could focus on optimizing the MLLM prompting and generation process or exploring distillation techniques to reduce the model size.
    * **Prompt Engineering:** While the prompts are presented, the specific process of arriving at these prompts, and their sensitivity, could be discussed in more detail. The performance is likely tightly coupled to prompt quality, raising concerns about robustness to unforeseen inputs.
    * **Negative Image Set Size:** The necessity of a "large number of negative images" to "ensure the extensive negative space" is a trade-off against computational efficiency and the possibility of including lower-quality examples that pollute the negative space. An analysis and justification of the number of negative images used is needed.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a compelling and well-executed approach to OOD detection using MLLMs. While it builds upon existing work, the specific combination of generating expressive negative sentences, visually similar negative labels, and adaptively weighting them constitutes a novel contribution. The performance gains are substantial, and the training-free nature is a significant advantage. The weaknesses mentioned above (reliance on initial detector, computational cost, prompt sensitivity, and negative image set size) are relevant but do not diminish the overall contribution significantly. Therefore, a score of 8 is justified. It's a solid improvement in OOD detection with practical relevance due to its zero-shot nature, but not entirely groundbreaking.
Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### World Model Implanting for Test-time Adaptation of Embodied Agents
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03956v1)
- **Authors**: Minjong Yoo, Jinwoo Jang, Sihyung Yoon, Honguk Woo
- **Abstract**: In embodied AI, a persistent challenge is enabling agents to robustly adapt to novel domains without requiring extensive data collection or retraining. To address this, we present a world model implanting framework (WorMI) that combines the reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs) with independently learned, domain-specific world models through test-time composition. By allowing seamless implantation and removal of the world models, the embodied agent's policy achieves and maintains cross-domain adaptability. In the WorMI framework, we employ a prototype-based world model retrieval approach, utilizing efficient trajectory-based abstract representation matching, to incorporate relevant models into test-time composition. We also develop a world-wise compound attention method that not only integrates the knowledge from the retrieved world models but also aligns their intermediate representations with the reasoning model's representation within the agent's policy. This framework design effectively fuses domain-specific knowledge from multiple world models, ensuring robust adaptation to unseen domains. We evaluate our WorMI on the VirtualHome and ALFWorld benchmarks, demonstrating superior zero-shot and few-shot performance compared to several LLM-based approaches across a range of unseen domains. These results highlight the frameworks potential for scalable, real-world deployment in embodied agent scenarios where adaptability and data efficiency are essential.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "World Model Implanting for Test-time Adaptation of Embodied Agents":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel framework called World Model Implanting (WorMI) to improve the test-time adaptation capabilities of embodied AI agents in unseen domains. WorMI leverages large language models (LLMs) by combining their reasoning abilities with independently learned, domain-specific world models. The core idea is to enable the agent to dynamically retrieve and "implant" relevant world models during test-time, allowing it to adapt to new environments without extensive retraining.  WorMI uses a prototype-based retrieval method to select relevant world models based on trajectory similarities.  A "world-wise compound attention" mechanism then integrates the knowledge from the retrieved world models, aligning their representations with the LLM-based reasoning model within the agent's policy. Experiments on VirtualHome and ALFWorld show that WorMI achieves superior zero-shot and few-shot performance compared to existing LLM-based approaches.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of dynamically implanting world models into an LLM-based agent is a novel approach to addressing the problem of test-time adaptation. The paper's key contributions are the specific mechanisms developed to realize this idea: the prototype-based retrieval and the world-wise compound attention. While both ideas build on existing techniques (e.g., prototype-based learning, attention mechanisms), their combination and application in the context of embodied agents is innovative. The hierarchical design allowing for both world-to-world integration and world-to-reasoning alignment is particularly notable.

*   **Significance:**  The problem of adapting embodied agents to new environments is a significant challenge in the field of AI. WorMI addresses this challenge by enabling agents to leverage existing knowledge through test-time composition, potentially leading to more robust and data-efficient systems. The empirical results, showing improved zero-shot and few-shot performance, support the significance of the approach.  The potential for real-world deployment is highlighted, especially in scenarios with environmental variations and diverse objectives.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear and well-motivated approach:** The paper clearly articulates the problem, proposes a solution (WorMI), and provides a strong rationale for its design.
    *   **Effective mechanisms:** The prototype-based retrieval and the world-wise compound attention are well-designed and contribute to the overall performance of the system.
    *   **Solid experimental results:** The experiments on VirtualHome and ALFWorld provide convincing evidence of the effectiveness of WorMI, compared to multiple baselines and across different domains.
    *   **Ablation studies:** The ablation studies further illuminate the contribution of key components within the WorMI framework. The scalability experiments are also interesting.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational overhead:** The paper acknowledges the computational cost associated with inferring over multiple world models. While prototype retrieval helps, the overhead could still be a limitation in real-time or resource-constrained settings. This needs further analysis.
    *   **Reliance on LLMs:**  The performance of WorMI is ultimately dependent on the quality and capabilities of the underlying LLM. This dependence might limit its performance in certain scenarios or require careful selection of an appropriate LLM. The paper mentions it but doesn't explore the limitations deeply.
    *   **Limited diversity in tasks/environments:** While VirtualHome and ALFWorld are valuable benchmarks, they may not fully capture the complexity and diversity of real-world environments. Evaluating WorMI on more diverse and challenging tasks would strengthen the claims of its broad applicability.
    *   **Incremental nature:** While novel in its combination, the components themselves are not revolutionary, but rather represent an intelligent integration of existing ideas.

*   **Potential Influence:** WorMI's dynamic model implantation framework could influence future research on embodied AI by promoting a shift towards more adaptable and composable systems. The prototype-based retrieval and world-wise attention mechanisms could also inspire new approaches to knowledge integration in other AI domains. However, more exploration of the limitations and scalability issues will be critical to wider adoption. The unlearning experiments present some interesting capabilities for future research.

* **Justification of Score**: The paper presents a novel and effective framework for improving the adaptability of embodied agents. The core concept of dynamic world model implantation, combined with the prototype-based retrieval and compound attention mechanism, offers a significant contribution to the field. However, the weaknesses relating to scalability, strong LLM dependence, the incremental nature of the methods, and the limited diversity of evaluation benchmarks prevents assigning a higher score. The experimental results are solid and highlight the performance improvements offered by WorMI.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### CANDY: Benchmarking LLMs' Limitations and Assistive Potential in Chinese Misinformation Fact-Checking
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03957v1)
- **Authors**: Ruiling Guo, Xinwei Yang, Chen Huang, Tong Zhang, Yong Hu
- **Abstract**: The effectiveness of large language models (LLMs) to fact-check misinformation remains uncertain, despite their growing use. To this end, we present CANDY, a benchmark designed to systematically evaluate the capabilities and limitations of LLMs in fact-checking Chinese misinformation. Specifically, we curate a carefully annotated dataset of ~20k instances. Our analysis shows that current LLMs exhibit limitations in generating accurate fact-checking conclusions, even when enhanced with chain-of-thought reasoning and few-shot prompting. To understand these limitations, we develop a taxonomy to categorize flawed LLM-generated explanations for their conclusions and identify factual fabrication as the most common failure mode. Although LLMs alone are unreliable for fact-checking, our findings indicate their considerable potential to augment human performance when deployed as assistive tools in scenarios. Our dataset and code can be accessed at https://github.com/SCUNLP/CANDY
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "CANDY: Benchmarking LLMs' Limitations and Assistive Potential in Chinese Misinformation Fact-Checking":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CANDY, a new benchmark dataset and framework for evaluating the capabilities and limitations of Large Language Models (LLMs) in fact-checking Chinese misinformation. The authors created CANDYSET, a curated dataset of approximately 20,000 news instances, including both misinformation and authentic news, with detailed evidence and annotations. They also developed a taxonomy to categorize flawed LLM-generated explanations. The benchmark includes three tasks: fact-checking conclusion, fact-checking explanation evaluation, and LLM-assisted fact-checking (a human-LLM interaction study). They benchmark sixteen LLMs and three Large Reasoning Models (LRMs), analyzing their performance and identifying key challenges such as hallucination, especially in time-sensitive and contamination-free scenarios, and a tendency for models to generate plausible-sounding misinformation. The human study suggests LLMs are more effective as assistive tools in fact-checking rather than as autonomous decision-makers.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper makes several significant contributions that provide novelty to the space of fact-checking:
    *   **Chinese Language Focus:** It specifically addresses the gap in resources for fact-checking *Chinese* misinformation. This is important given the scale of Chinese online content and the specific cultural and linguistic nuances involved.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation Framework:** CANDY provides a multi-faceted evaluation.  It doesn't just look at the final "correct/incorrect" answer. It examines *explanations*, categorizes *errors*, and investigates *human-LLM interaction*. This holistic approach is a major strength.
    *   **Taxonomy of Flaws:** The proposed taxonomy for categorizing flawed LLM explanations is a valuable tool for detailed error analysis. It goes beyond simply saying "the model was wrong" and helps pinpoint *why* the model failed.
    *   **Human-in-the-Loop Study:** Including a human study to assess LLMs as *assistive* tools is significant.  It acknowledges that completely automated fact-checking is not yet reliable and explores a more realistic application scenario.
*   **Significance:** The findings have several important implications for the field:
    *   **Highlighting Limitations:** The paper clearly demonstrates that current LLMs, despite advancements, are not reliable enough for independent fact-checking, particularly regarding time-sensitive and emerging misinformation.
    *   **Identifying Failure Modes:**  Pinpointing factual hallucination as a major issue is crucial for guiding future research towards mitigating this problem.
    *   **Informing System Design:**  The study supports the idea of using LLMs as assistive tools in a human-centered fact-checking pipeline. This informs the design of practical fact-checking systems that leverage LLMs effectively.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Thorough Analysis:** The paper presents a rigorous analysis, using a sizable dataset and multiple evaluation metrics.
    *   **Well-Organized:** The paper is well-structured and clearly written, making it easy to follow the methodology and findings.
    *   **Practical Implications:** The findings offer valuable guidance for researchers and practitioners in the field of misinformation detection.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Prompt Sensitivity:** The authors acknowledge that the findings are sensitive to the prompts used, a common limitation in LLM research. While multiple prompts were tested, there's no guarantee that even better prompts wouldn't yield different results.
    *   **Limited LLMs for explanation analysis:** There are large number of LLMs for Fact-checking Conclusion Task, while the explanation analysis task was limited by the cost of manual labeling.

*   **Potential Influence:** The CANDY benchmark is likely to become a valuable resource for researchers working on fact-checking, especially those focused on the Chinese language. The taxonomy of errors and the insights from the human study can inform the development of more robust and reliable fact-checking systems. The negative findings about LLMs alone, while important, might be seen as reiterating known problems, but the detail and specificity in this Chinese context contribute additional weight.

**Overall Score and Justification:**

I assign a score of **8/10**.

The paper provides a novel and thorough investigation into LLMs' fact-checking capabilities, specifically focusing on the critical, yet under-explored, domain of Chinese misinformation. The CANDY benchmark offers a valuable tool for future research, and the insights about LLMs' limitations and their potential as assistive tools are significant. While the prompt sensitivity is a known limitation and the LLM number for explanation limited by cost, the paper's strengths, particularly the comprehensive evaluation framework, the error taxonomy, and the inclusion of a human study, justify a high score, reflecting its valuable contribution to the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 0/10

### Exploring NLP Benchmarks in an Extremely Low-Resource Setting
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03962v1)
- **Authors**: Ulin Nuha, Adam Jatowt
- **Abstract**: The effectiveness of Large Language Models (LLMs) diminishes for extremely low-resource languages, such as indigenous languages, primarily due to the lack of labeled data. Despite growing interest, the availability of high-quality natural language processing (NLP) datasets for these languages remains limited, making it difficult to develop robust language technologies. This paper addresses such gap by focusing on Ladin, an endangered Romance language, specifically targeting the Val Badia variant. Leveraging a small set of parallel Ladin-Italian sentence pairs, we create synthetic datasets for sentiment analysis and multiple-choice question answering (MCQA) by translating monolingual Italian data. To ensure linguistic quality and reliability, we apply rigorous filtering and back-translation procedures in our method. We further demonstrate that incorporating these synthetic datasets into machine translation training leads to substantial improvements over existing Italian-Ladin translation baselines. Our contributions include the first publicly available sentiment analysis and MCQA datasets for Ladin, establishing foundational resources that can support broader NLP research and downstream applications for this underrepresented language.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of developing NLP resources for Ladin, an extremely low-resource language in Northern Italy.  The authors create synthetic datasets for sentiment analysis (SA) and multiple-choice question answering (MCQA) by translating Italian data into Ladin. They employ rigorous filtering and back-translation techniques to ensure data quality. The authors demonstrate that incorporating these synthetic datasets improves machine translation performance between Italian and Ladin and establish the first publicly available SA and MCQA datasets for Ladin. They conduct several experiments using various language models, including LLMs (LLaMA, GPT-4) and encoder-decoder architectures (MBART, NLLB).

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in creating the first publicly available SA and MCQA datasets for Ladin. This is a significant step as the availability of resources is a primary bottleneck for NLP development in extremely low-resource settings. The application of rigorous filtering and back-translation to generate synthetic data is not entirely new, but the specific combination of techniques and the careful parameter tuning for Ladin language makes this a nontrivial effort.

*   **Significance:** The paper directly tackles a real-world problem—the lack of NLP resources for endangered languages. By providing these datasets and improving translation, the authors are contributing to the preservation and accessibility of the Ladin language.  The improvement in machine translation, while incremental, shows that synthetic data augmentation can be effective even in extremely low-resource scenarios. The study validates the potential for using similar strategies for other under-represented languages. The impact of the study on the Ladin speaking community is not addressed, representing a critical weakness. The study would be made more significant if the resources were tested within the target community of speakers to ensure their utility.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper provides a clear and well-documented methodology for creating synthetic datasets.
    *   The experimental setup is comprehensive, using several models and metrics.
    *   The authors perform a qualitative error analysis, providing insights into the strengths and weaknesses of the translation models.
    *   The introduction is very effective at motivating the problem and highlighting the need for resources for ELRLs.
    *   The paper addresses the scarcity of resources by creating two benchmark datasets, with the aim of stimulating further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The performance gains from incorporating synthetic data in MT, although positive, are not dramatically large and might be considered incremental.
    *   The performance on the MCQA task remains low even after augmentation, which somewhat limits the immediate practical impact of that dataset. The explanation attributes the MCQA tasks to a number of characteristics (small dataset and topical coverage), indicating that further refinement of data construction is needed.
    *   While the synthetic datasets are evaluated using automatic metrics, a human evaluation of the quality of the generated SA and MCQA data would further strengthen the validation of these resources.
    *   The qualitative analysis of the synthetic data, while helpful, remains limited and does not fully address how the quality of the SA and MCQA data may impact the down-stream tasks.
    *   The paper doesn't address the downstream impact of the generated resources on the Ladin speaking community.
    *   While some sources of error are explained, the study could be improved through an error analysis detailing the limitations of the LLM on MT tasks.
    *   Statistical significance tests are not done on the results, indicating a lack of rigor and statistical validation.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to stimulate further research in NLP for Ladin and other extremely low-resource languages. The datasets will likely be used by other researchers as benchmarks.  The methodology can be adapted and applied to other similar languages.

**Score:** 6.5

**Justification:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution by creating new datasets for an under-represented language, but performance gains are incremental. The impact on downstream applications within the Ladin speaking community is not yet demonstrated. While the methodology is sound, the performance on the MCQA task and a complete lack of statistical analysis represent significant weaknesses. The improvements in Machine Translation alone are not enough to justify a score higher than 6.5. The datasets represent significant contributions to the space, but further work is needed to determine if these are truly impactful in the end application.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### NeuroBreak: Unveil Internal Jailbreak Mechanisms in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03985v1)
- **Authors**: Chuhan Zhang, Ye Zhang, Bowen Shi, Yuyou Gan, Tianyu Du, Shouling Ji, Dazhan Deng, Yingcai Wu
- **Abstract**: In deployment and application, large language models (LLMs) typically undergo safety alignment to prevent illegal and unethical outputs. However, the continuous advancement of jailbreak attack techniques, designed to bypass safety mechanisms with adversarial prompts, has placed increasing pressure on the security defenses of LLMs. Strengthening resistance to jailbreak attacks requires an in-depth understanding of the security mechanisms and vulnerabilities of LLMs. However, the vast number of parameters and complex structure of LLMs make analyzing security weaknesses from an internal perspective a challenging task. This paper presents NeuroBreak, a top-down jailbreak analysis system designed to analyze neuron-level safety mechanisms and mitigate vulnerabilities. We carefully design system requirements through collaboration with three experts in the field of AI security. The system provides a comprehensive analysis of various jailbreak attack methods. By incorporating layer-wise representation probing analysis, NeuroBreak offers a novel perspective on the model's decision-making process throughout its generation steps. Furthermore, the system supports the analysis of critical neurons from both semantic and functional perspectives, facilitating a deeper exploration of security mechanisms. We conduct quantitative evaluations and case studies to verify the effectiveness of our system, offering mechanistic insights for developing next-generation defense strategies against evolving jailbreak attacks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the NeuroBreak paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces NeuroBreak, a visual analytics system designed to help understand and mitigate jailbreak attacks on large language models (LLMs). These attacks bypass safety mechanisms with adversarial prompts, inducing prohibited responses.  NeuroBreak offers a multi-granular analysis pipeline, starting with an overall assessment of the LLM's security and utility, progressing to layer-wise representation probing to identify semantic shifts, and culminating in neuron-level analysis to pinpoint critical neurons responsible for jailbreak vulnerabilities. The system combines semantic-aware and functionality-aware analysis, revealing neuron collaboration patterns and allowing users to explore the dynamic evolution of jailbreak attacks. NeuroBreak supports targeted fine-tuning based on identified vulnerabilities and is validated through case studies and quantitative evaluations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a visually-driven approach to understanding the internal mechanisms of LLMs under jailbreak attacks, which is somewhat novel. While prior work has looked at interpretability and security, NeuroBreak’s combination of multi-granular analysis, including layer-wise probing and neuron-level analysis through a visual interface, is a significant contribution.  Specifically, the use of a multi-layer radial layout for neuron analysis and the integration of semantic and functional perspectives is notable.  However, many components used are borrowed (SNIP score, linear probing).  The core novelty lies in the system's design integrating existing techniques rather than fundamentally novel techniques.
*   **Significance:** Jailbreak attacks are a critical concern for LLM deployment, and understanding their inner workings is essential for developing robust defenses. NeuroBreak offers a practical tool for researchers and practitioners to analyze vulnerabilities, identify critical neurons, and implement targeted fine-tuning strategies.  The case studies demonstrate that the system can provide actionable insights for improving LLM security.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Comprehensive approach: Multi-granular analysis provides a holistic view of jailbreak attacks.
    *   Visual Analytics: The user interface facilitates exploration and understanding of complex data.
    *   Practical application: Demonstrated effectiveness through case studies and targeted fine-tuning experiments.
    *   Collaboration with AI Security Experts: Requirement analysis is driven by the experts that will actually use the system.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Dataset limitations: Evaluation relies on SALAD-Bench, which may not cover the full spectrum of real-world attacks or cross-lingual prompts.
    *   Linear Probing: Limited to exploring linear relationships within layers and might overlook non-linear semantic shifts.
    *   Generalizability: The effectiveness is demonstrated on Llama-3-8B. Performance with other models or scales might differ.
    *   Limited Quantitative Evaluation: While the quantitative evaluation shows improved security with fine-tuning, it could be expanded to compare NeuroBreak against other established interpretability methods or defense strategies to more explicitly show its utility.
    *   Need for Broader Availability: While the paper discusses benefits of open-sourcing and deployment, the software isn't available to be widely used and assessed.
*   **Potential Impact:** NeuroBreak has the potential to influence the development of next-generation defense strategies against evolving jailbreak attacks. It can provide a framework for understanding the inner workings of LLMs under attack, enabling the design of more robust and targeted safety mechanisms. However, its impact will depend on broader adoption and further refinements to address its limitations. The visual insights provided are strong and useful but the overall improvement is more incremental rather than a step-change.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

The paper makes a contribution in the area of LLM security by presenting a system to understand and potentially mitigate jailbreak attacks. The novelty lies in the integration of visual analytics with existing interpretability and fine-tuning techniques rather than a fundamental breakthrough in any individual technique. While NeuroBreak offers practical benefits and provides actionable insights, its dependence on established datasets and the limitations of its analysis methods slightly lowers the overall score.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 6/10

### Divergence-Kernel method for linear responses and diffusion models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03992v1)
- **Authors**: Angxiu Ni
- **Abstract**: We derive the divergence-kernel formula for the linear response (parameter-derivative of marginal or stationary distributions) of random dynamical systems, and formally pass to the continuous-time limit. Our formula works for multiplicative and parameterized noise over any period of time; it does not require hyperbolicity. Then we derive a pathwise Monte-Carlo algorithm for linear responses. With this, we propose a forward-only diffusion generative model and test on simple problems.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary, critical evaluation, and score for the paper based on its content.

**Summary:**

The paper presents a divergence-kernel method for computing linear responses (parameter derivatives of marginal or stationary distributions) of random dynamical systems. It derives a formal divergence-kernel formula applicable to continuous-time stochastic differential equations (SDEs) with multiplicative and parameterized noise, without requiring hyperbolicity. A pathwise Monte-Carlo algorithm is proposed for computing these linear responses. The paper also introduces a forward-only diffusion generative model that leverages this method for gradient-based optimization of marginal distributions concerning diffusion coefficients, aiming to reduce the KL-divergence between the model's output and real-world data. Numerical experiments are conducted on a 1D system with multiplicative noise and the 40D Lorenz 96 model to demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method.  Finally, it applies it to build a generative model.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Novelty:*

The primary novelty lies in deriving a divergence-kernel formula for linear responses of SDEs applicable to multiplicative and parameterized noise *without requiring hyperbolicity*. This is a significant departure from some prior methods that rely on hyperbolicity or are less suited for multiplicative noise. Furthermore, the introduction of a *forward-only* diffusion generative model directly optimizing diffusion coefficients using the derived gradients is a novel approach, differing from common backpropagation or score-matching-based methods.  While the individual components (divergence kernel methods, gradient-based optimization, diffusion models) are not entirely new, their combination and specific application to multiplicative noise and direct diffusion coefficient optimization constitute a novel contribution. The examples also offer something novel in the sense that previous methods cannot solve them.

*Significance:*

The significance of the work stems from the potential to:

1.  **Enable gradient-based optimization of diffusion processes**: The divergence-kernel formula provides a tool to optimize marginal or stationary densities with respect to diffusion coefficients directly, allowing for the creation of more tailored diffusion generative models.

2.  **Handle multiplicative noise effectively**: Many real-world systems exhibit multiplicative noise, and this method offers a way to model and optimize them. The examples offered are proof of it.

3.  **Avoid hyperbolicity assumptions**: By removing the need for hyperbolicity, the method can be applied to a broader range of systems, including those exhibiting complex, non-hyperbolic dynamics.

4. **Improve generative diffusion models**: The proposed forward-only approach can potentially reduce memory costs compared to models relying on backward propagation.

*Weaknesses:*

1.  **Formal derivations**: The paper emphasizes that the continuous-time limit derivation is formal, lacking rigorous mathematical proof. This limits the practical applicability in scenarios where theoretical guarantees are necessary. This represents a big problem for the paper because there are numerous formal derivations of linear responses, without being actually useful for practical purposes.
2.  **Numerical results**:  The numerical experiments, while demonstrating the method's feasibility, are relatively simple. Showing the method's success in high-dimensional or complex datasets would significantly increase its impact. More comprehensive experiments are needed.
3.  **Computational cost**:  The paper mentions potential computational costs, especially when the noise is small or the contraction is strong. A more thorough analysis of the computational complexity and efficiency would be beneficial. In particular, it is known that in high-dimensional spaces Montecarlo can become very costly.
4.  **Assumptions on regularity**: The assumptions on the regularity of the initial density, probability kernel, drift, and diffusion may limit the applicability of the method to certain systems.

*Impact:*

If the formal derivations can be made rigorous, and the method proves effective in more complex and high-dimensional settings, it could significantly influence the field of diffusion generative models and optimization of stochastic dynamical systems. It could allow for the development of more refined and tailored models that capture intricate dynamics and handle multiplicative noise effectively.

**Score:** 6

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel approach with the potential for significant impact in the field of diffusion generative models. The ability to directly optimize diffusion coefficients through gradient descent, the handling of multiplicative noise, and the removal of hyperbolicity constraints are valuable contributions. However, the lack of rigorous proof for the continuous-time limit, the limited scope of the numerical experiments, and concerns about computational cost prevent a higher score. While the *idea* is strong and the *potential* impact is high, the current state of the paper, with formal derivations and limited demonstration, warrants a score of 6. Future work rigorously proving the theorems and demonstrating the method's efficacy on more challenging and realistic datasets will significantly increase its value and impact. There are a few papers proposing interesting theories, but never becoming popular because they are difficult to make effective. The value of this paper will be the practical applications, if the authors can do so.

- **Classification**: math.DS
- **Score**: 6/10

### RTQA : Recursive Thinking for Complex Temporal Knowledge Graph Question Answering with Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03995v1)
- **Authors**: Zhaoyan Gong, Juan Li, Zhiqiang Liu, Lei Liang, Huajun Chen, Wen Zhang
- **Abstract**: Current temporal knowledge graph question answering (TKGQA) methods primarily focus on implicit temporal constraints, lacking the capability of handling more complex temporal queries, and struggle with limited reasoning abilities and error propagation in decomposition frameworks. We propose RTQA, a novel framework to address these challenges by enhancing reasoning over TKGs without requiring training. Following recursive thinking, RTQA recursively decomposes questions into sub-problems, solves them bottom-up using LLMs and TKG knowledge, and employs multi-path answer aggregation to improve fault tolerance. RTQA consists of three core components: the Temporal Question Decomposer, the Recursive Solver, and the Answer Aggregator. Experiments on MultiTQ and TimelineKGQA benchmarks demonstrate significant Hits@1 improvements in "Multiple" and "Complex" categories, outperforming state-of-the-art methods. Our code and data are available at https://github.com/zjukg/RTQA.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "RTQA: Recursive Thinking for Complex Temporal Knowledge Graph Question Answering with Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RTQA, a novel framework for temporal knowledge graph question answering (TKGQA) designed to address complex queries that involve implicit temporal constraints, multi-constraint combinations, and multi-hop reasoning.  RTQA leverages a "divide and conquer" strategy, recursively decomposing complex questions into simpler sub-problems, solving them in a bottom-up manner using Large Language Models (LLMs) and TKG knowledge.  A key component is a multi-path answer aggregation module which enhances fault tolerance by combining answers from both sub-questions and the original question, reducing error propagation. Experiments on MultiTQ and TimelineKGQA benchmarks demonstrate significant improvements, particularly in "Multiple" and "Complex" categories, outperforming state-of-the-art methods. The framework is training-free and "plug-and-play," requiring no computational overhead and adapting to various large models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Problem Relevance:** The paper addresses a critical and challenging problem in TKGQA – handling complex temporal reasoning, an area where existing methods often fall short.  The examples provided effectively illustrate the limitations of current approaches.
*   **Novelty:** The recursive decomposition strategy is not entirely novel in the general QA field, but its application to TKGQA, specifically with the integration of LLMs and the emphasis on *temporal* constraints, is a worthwhile contribution. The multi-path answer aggregation is a particularly clever design element that addresses the inherent weakness of decomposition approaches (error propagation).
*   **Technical Soundness:** The paper presents a well-defined framework with clear descriptions of each component (Temporal Question Decomposer, Recursive Solver, Answer Aggregator).  The algorithms and processes are formalized.
*   **Experimental Validation:** The experiments are comprehensive, utilizing two established and challenging benchmarks.  The results convincingly demonstrate the superiority of RTQA over strong baselines, particularly in the more difficult query categories.  The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component.
*   **Plug-and-play and training free**: Avoiding re-training is a valuable consideration in the LLM era, making the solution more accessible and adaptable.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Reliance on LLMs:**  The framework's performance is heavily dependent on the underlying LLM's reasoning capabilities.  While the paper mentions limitations with smaller models, it doesn't fully explore the sensitivity of RTQA to LLM choice (beyond a high-level comparison across models).  A more in-depth analysis of *why* certain LLMs perform better with RTQA would be beneficial.
*   **Limited Generalizability:**  The paper admits that RTQA is primarily tailored for TKGQA.  While it performs better on timelines, the reliance on curated, temporal knowledge sources may limit its effectiveness in more open-domain or dynamically changing environments.
*   **Practical Concerns**: The reliance on an API (GPT-4o) creates concerns about real-world cost and availability. An open source LLM could have been integrated in the experiments in order to reduce dependence. Furthermore, the number of API calls necessary may create a very high latency at inference time, limiting the practical usability of the proposed approach.
*   **Ablation Study Details**: While the ablation studies are valuable, more detail on how "decomposition" and "multi-answer" were disabled is necessary for a full understanding.

**Significance:**

RTQA represents a significant step forward in TKGQA, particularly for queries with complex temporal constraints.  The framework's design principles (recursive decomposition, multi-path aggregation) could be adopted in other QA domains. The emphasis on a training-free, plug-and-play approach is also practically relevant. The improvements demonstrated over strong baselines validate the effectiveness of the proposed techniques.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper provides an impactful solution to a relevant and well-defined problem in temporal knowledge graph question answering. While recursive decomposition has been explored in other contexts, the integration of LLMs and the novel multi-path answer aggregation significantly improves performance and addresses a crucial weakness of decompositional methods. The experimental results are solid, and the ablation studies provide good insights. However, the reliance on LLMs, limited generalizability beyond the TKGQA domain, and practical concerns limit the overall score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### AutoPBO: LLM-powered Optimization for Local Search PBO Solvers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04007v1)
- **Authors**: Jinyuan Li, Yi Chu, Yiwen Sun, Mengchuan Zou, Shaowei Cai
- **Abstract**: Pseudo-Boolean Optimization (PBO) provides a powerful framework for modeling combinatorial problems through pseudo-Boolean (PB) constraints. Local search solvers have shown excellent performance in PBO solving, and their efficiency is highly dependent on their internal heuristics to guide the search. Still, their design often requires significant expert effort and manual tuning in practice. While Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated potential in automating algorithm design, their application to optimizing PBO solvers remains unexplored. In this work, we introduce AutoPBO, a novel LLM-powered framework to automatically enhance PBO local search solvers.
