The paper "TauGenNet: Plasma-Driven Tau PET Image Synthesis via Text-Guided 3D Diffusion Models" proposes a method for generating synthetic 3D tau PET images using a text-guided diffusion model.  The model is conditioned on both structural MRI and plasma p-tau217 measurements. The plasma p-tau217 measurement is encoded as a textual prompt using CLIP, while the MRI data provides anatomical constraints. The framework is trained and evaluated on ADNI data. The results show that the generated tau PET images are realistic, clinically meaningful, and reflect disease progression consistent with known AD pathology. The authors argue that their approach can augment limited tau PET datasets, provide a cost-effective alternative to in-vivo PET imaging, and support disease progression simulations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a novel approach for synthesizing 3D tau PET images using both MRI and plasma p-tau217 measurements.  Using plasma p-tau217 as a condition for PET synthesis is a promising idea, given the invasiveness and cost limitations of PET imaging. The use of a text-guided 3D diffusion model for this purpose is also a relatively new application.  Specifically, using CLIP to encode p-tau217 value is insightful.
    *   **Methodology:** The methodological details seem reasonable, and the framework's architecture (autoencoder pretraining, forward/reverse diffusion processes, cross-attention) is well-established.  The use of a 3D diffusion model is appropriate given the inherent 3D nature of PET data, addressing the limitations of the authors' previous 2D work.
    *   **Results:** The experimental results provide some evidence that the proposed method can generate realistic and meaningful tau PET images, capturing the known disease progression patterns in AD. The comparison of using plasma alone vs plasma+MRI shows the importance of structural constraints. The results are generally compelling. The visualization of generated tau PET across a gradient of p-tau217 values is a good demonstration.
    *   **Significance:** The potential impact of the work is significant. If validated further, it could significantly improve AD research by mitigating the limited availability of tau PET data, supporting larger machine learning studies, enabling disease progression simulations, and potentially offering a cost-effective alternative for visualization of tau pathology.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Dataset:** The dataset size (360 paired images) is relatively small for training a complex deep learning model. This raises concerns about the generalizability and robustness of the approach. More comprehensive validation is needed.
    *   **Quantification & Validation:** While visual results appear good, a more rigorous quantitative validation is crucial. The MSE calculations by regions and plasma levels are a decent start, but other metrics (e.g., structural similarity index (SSIM), Frechet Inception Distance (FID) or its variants adapted for medical images) could provide a more complete picture. Clinical validation involving comparing synthetic PET images of patients with their clinical outcomes may also be considered.
    *   **Limited Scope:**  The model only considers p-tau217 and MRI data as conditioning inputs. Integrating additional biomarkers, clinical information, demographic factors, and even amyloid PET data could potentially improve the performance and clinical relevance of the framework.
    *   **Variance Reduction:** The paper acknowledges that the generated images have lower variance than the real data, likely due to limited model inputs. This means there may be an "over-smoothing" of tau deposition pattern.
    *   **Diagnostic Applicability Unclear:** The paper acknowledges that the generated PET images are *not* suitable for clinical diagnosis in their current form, due to limited dynamic range and a higher risk of prediction error than real images. This limits the direct applicability of the generated data.

*   **Novelty and Significance:** This work presents a novel combination of techniques to tackle a relevant problem. By creatively using the CLIP model in combination with plasma biomarker levels and 3D diffusion models, this work shows some novelty. While there are limitations as noted, further improvements to the current method has the potential to significantly augment AD research.

**Overall Score and Justification:**

Given the strengths and weaknesses, I would assign a score of **6.5**. The work shows good promise and introduces a novel approach to tau PET image synthesis with clinical relevance. However, limitations such as the relatively small dataset, need for comprehensive validation, and limited scope prevent this work from receiving a higher score. With more data, better validation strategies, inclusion of further biomarkers, and a clinical evaluation, this score could be increased.

Score: 6.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Inverse IFEval: Can LLMs Unlearn Stubborn Training Conventions to Follow Real Instructions?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04292v1)
- **Authors**: Qinyan Zhang, Xinping Lei, Ruijie Miao, Yu Fu, Haojie Fan, Le Chang, Jiafan Hou, Dingling Zhang, Zhongfei Hou, Ziqiang Yang, Changxin Pu, Fei Hu, Jingkai Liu, Mengyun Liu, Yang Liu, Xiang Gao, Jiaheng Liu, Tong Yang, Zaiyuan Wang, Ge Zhang, Wenhao Huang
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) achieve strong performance on diverse tasks but often exhibit cognitive inertia, struggling to follow instructions that conflict with the standardized patterns learned during supervised fine-tuning (SFT). To evaluate this limitation, we propose Inverse IFEval, a benchmark that measures models Counter-intuitive Abilitytheir capacity to override training-induced biases and comply with adversarial instructions. Inverse IFEval introduces eight types of such challenges, including Question Correction, Intentional Textual Flaws, Code without Comments, and Counterfactual Answering. Using a human-in-the-loop pipeline, we construct a dataset of 1012 high-quality Chinese and English questions across 23 domains, evaluated under an optimized LLM-as-a-Judge framework. Experiments on existing leading LLMs demonstrate the necessity of our proposed Inverse IFEval benchmark. Our findings emphasize that future alignment efforts should not only pursue fluency and factual correctness but also account for adaptability under unconventional contexts. We hope that Inverse IFEval serves as both a diagnostic tool and a foundation for developing methods that mitigate cognitive inertia, reduce overfitting to narrow patterns, and ultimately enhance the instruction-following reliability of LLMs in diverse and unpredictable real-world scenarios.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "Inverse IFEval: Can LLMs Unlearn Stubborn Training Conventions to Follow Real Instructions?" paper, based on the provided OCR text.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Inverse IFEval," a new benchmark designed to evaluate Large Language Models' (LLMs) ability to follow counter-intuitive instructions that deviate from standard training paradigms. The authors argue that LLMs often exhibit "cognitive inertia," struggling to override ingrained biases learned during supervised fine-tuning (SFT). Inverse IFEval consists of 1012 high-quality Chinese and English questions across 23 diverse domains, spanning eight categories of "inverse instructions" (e.g., Question Correction, Intentional Textual Flaws, Code without Comments).  The benchmark uses an optimized LLM-as-a-Judge framework for evaluation.  Experiments on leading LLMs demonstrate the necessity of this new benchmark and highlight the importance of adaptability under unconventional contexts. The authors hope Inverse IFEval will serve as a diagnostic tool and foundation for developing methods to mitigate cognitive inertia and enhance the reliability of LLMs in diverse scenarios.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The key novelty lies in the explicit focus on *counter-intuitive* instructions. Existing benchmarks like MMLU, IFEval, and others primarily assess factual knowledge or general instruction-following capabilities under standard conditions. By systematically inverting common training patterns, the paper introduces a distinct and important dimension for evaluating LLM robustness. The eight proposed categories of inverse instructions are well-defined and provide a structured approach to testing this ability.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the practical implications of addressing "cognitive inertia." LLMs are increasingly deployed in real-world applications where they encounter unconventional user requests, ambiguous inputs, or dynamically shifting requirements. A model that can faithfully follow instructions even when they contradict its training data is crucial for reliable performance. Inverse IFEval sheds light on this limitation and motivates future research into alignment methods that prioritize adaptability.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined Benchmark:** The Inverse IFEval benchmark is thoughtfully constructed with a clear methodology for data collection (human-in-the-loop, expert review), diverse domains, and multiple instruction types.
    *   **Practical Relevance:** The identified "cognitive inertia" is a genuine issue that affects the usability of LLMs in many real-world scenarios.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The paper presents a thorough experimental analysis across multiple languages, model families, and the different instruction types, providing valuable insights into the limitations of current alignment methods.
    *   **Clear Articulation of a Problem:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of models struggling to deviate from learned patterns, which is often overlooked in the pursuit of factual correctness.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Artificiality of Instructions:** The instructions *are* deliberately artificial. While the paper acknowledges this, it's a potential limitation. It's essential to understand the *correlation* between performance on these synthetic tasks and robustness in genuinely unexpected, naturally occurring user interactions.
    *   **Dependency on LLM-as-a-Judge:**  The reliability of the benchmark hinges on the accuracy of the LLM-as-a-Judge framework. While the authors report achieving 98% accuracy, it's important to acknowledge the potential for bias and the need for continuous refinement of this evaluation method. The evaluation rubrics are standardized, but LLMs also have to follow the given complex directions.
    *   **Limited Scope of Solutions:** The paper primarily focuses on *identifying* the problem rather than proposing concrete solutions. This is understandable for an initial benchmark paper, but future research should build upon this work to develop methods for mitigating cognitive inertia.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in LLM alignment by shifting the focus from purely factual correctness to adaptability and robustness. It could also lead to the development of new training techniques and model architectures that are less prone to cognitive inertia.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7.5**.

**Rationale:**

The paper introduces a novel and valuable benchmark that addresses an important, yet often overlooked, limitation of LLMs. The methodical data collection and comprehensive evaluation contribute significantly to our understanding of LLM behavior. The identification of "cognitive inertia" is a key contribution. However, the reliance on artificial instructions and the lack of concrete solutions prevent it from achieving a higher score. It is a strong foundation for future research but needs further validation with real-world scenarios and development of effective mitigation strategies. The potential impact on future alignment efforts is significant.

Score: 7.5
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Facts Fade Fast: Evaluating Memorization of Outdated Medical Knowledge in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04304v1)
- **Authors**: Juraj Vladika, Mahdi Dhaini, Florian Matthes
- **Abstract**: The growing capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) show significant potential to enhance healthcare by assisting medical researchers and physicians. However, their reliance on static training data is a major risk when medical recommendations evolve with new research and developments. When LLMs memorize outdated medical knowledge, they can provide harmful advice or fail at clinical reasoning tasks. To investigate this problem, we introduce two novel question-answering (QA) datasets derived from systematic reviews: MedRevQA (16,501 QA pairs covering general biomedical knowledge) and MedChangeQA (a subset of 512 QA pairs where medical consensus has changed over time). Our evaluation of eight prominent LLMs on the datasets reveals consistent reliance on outdated knowledge across all models. We additionally analyze the influence of obsolete pre-training data and training strategies to explain this phenomenon and propose future directions for mitigation, laying the groundwork for developing more current and reliable medical AI systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Facts Fade Fast: Evaluating Memorization of Outdated Medical Knowledge in Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses a critical safety concern related to the use of Large Language Models (LLMs) in healthcare: the memorization and subsequent reliance on outdated medical knowledge.  The authors introduce two novel question-answering (QA) datasets derived from Cochrane systematic reviews: MedRevQA (general biomedical knowledge) and MedChangeQA (a subset where medical consensus has changed over time).  They evaluate eight prominent LLMs on these datasets, demonstrating consistent reliance on outdated knowledge across all models. Furthermore, the paper explores the influence of obsolete pre-training data and training strategies on this phenomenon and proposes future mitigation directions. The research aims to contribute to developing more current and reliable medical AI systems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper introduces a novel and important problem framing - the explicit evaluation of outdated medical knowledge memorization in LLMs. The creation of the MedRevQA and MedChangeQA datasets is a significant contribution, as it provides a standardized benchmark for assessing this critical aspect of LLM performance. While temporal QA datasets exist, focusing specifically on the medical domain and knowledge decay in this way is relatively novel. Existing work has also not focused on QA datasets built from systematic reviews in this specific context. The idea to generate questions using objectives sections of SRs is interesting since it is more in line with the question these reviews try to address.

*   **Significance:**  The problem investigated is highly significant.  LLMs are increasingly being deployed in healthcare, and reliance on outdated knowledge could have serious consequences for patient safety and clinical decision-making.  The paper's findings highlight a major risk that needs to be addressed before widespread adoption of LLMs in medical settings. The paper demonstrates the prevalence of this issue across various popular LLMs (GPT, Mistral, Llama, etc), which stresses the importance of the issue across different LLM architectures and training regimes.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined problem:**  The paper clearly articulates the problem of outdated medical knowledge in LLMs and its potential risks.
    *   **Rigorous methodology:** The use of Cochrane systematic reviews as a source of ground truth is a strong methodological choice, given the high quality and rigorous standards of these reviews.
    *   **Empirical validation:** The evaluation of multiple LLMs on the newly created datasets provides strong empirical evidence for the existence of the problem.
    *   **In-depth analysis:** The paper goes beyond simply demonstrating the problem and explores potential causes, such as obsolete pre-training data.
    *   **Discussion of mitigation strategies:**  The paper proposes future directions for mitigating the problem, such as using retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) and continual learning. The discussion of inspection of OLMo data is insightful and suggests one cause of this problem.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Semi-automatic dataset creation:** The dataset construction relies on LLMs for question generation. Although the human inspection shows acceptable error rates, there is potential for introducing bias or inaccuracies. A deeper evaluation with medical experts would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Limited mitigation exploration:**  While the paper proposes mitigation strategies, it does not extensively test or compare them. The simple RAG improvement included in the appendix scratches the surface. Further exploration of these strategies would be valuable.
    *   **Cutoff dates of LLMs:** The analysis is limited by the pre-training cutoff date of the various LLMs, which means they may not incorporate the very latest updates.
    *   **Label Accuracy Concerns:** The paper mentions that the label set generated using the model was inaccurate around 5-8% of the time and suggests human error in this annotation process. This raises a flag since the labels may be inaccurate, which can negatively impact the analysis.
    *   **Lack of comparison to other QA methods**: The method of generating Q/A labels could be compared to other baselines to better understand its relative strengths and weaknesses.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to significantly influence the development and deployment of LLMs in healthcare.  It raises awareness of a critical safety concern and provides a valuable benchmark for evaluating and mitigating this risk.  The proposed mitigation strategies could guide future research in this area.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper makes a significant contribution by identifying and quantifying a real-world problem with LLMs in a safety-critical domain. It also introduces a valuable dataset that enables further research in this area. However, the limitations in the dataset creation and mitigation exploration slightly reduce the score. Given these considerations, the paper's high significance and solid methodology warrant a score of:

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Learning Optimal Crew Dispatch for Grid Restoration Following an Earthquake
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04308v1)
- **Authors**: Farshad Amani, Faezeh Ardali, Amin Kargarian
- **Abstract**: Post-disaster crew dispatch is a critical but computationally intensive task. Traditional mixed-integer linear programming methods often require minutes to several hours to compute solutions, leading to delays that hinder timely decision-making in highly dynamic restoration environments. To address this challenge, we propose a novel learning-based framework that integrates transformer architectures with deep reinforcement learning (DRL) to deliver near real-time decision support without compromising solution quality. Crew dispatch is formulated as a sequential decision-making problem under uncertainty, where transformers capture high-dimensional system states and temporal dependencies, while DRL enables adaptive and scalable decision-making. Earthquake-induced distribution network damage is first characterized using established seismic standards, followed by a scenario generation and reduction pipeline that aggregates probable outcomes into a single geospatial impact map. Conditioned on this map, the proposed framework generates second-level dispatch strategies, trained offline on simulated and historical events and deployed online for rapid response. In addition to substantial runtime improvements, the proposed method enhances system resilience by enabling faster and more effective recovery and restoration. Case studies, particularly on the 2869-bus European gas and power network, demonstrate that the method substantially accelerates restoration while maintaining high-quality solutions, underscoring its potential for practical deployment in large-scale disaster response.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper introduces a novel deep reinforcement learning (DRL) framework that combines transformer architectures for optimizing crew dispatch strategies in the aftermath of an earthquake. The method aims to provide near real-time decision support for grid restoration while maintaining solution quality. It formulates crew dispatch as a sequential decision-making problem under uncertainty. Earthquake-induced damage is modeled using seismic standards, and scenario generation aggregates potential outcomes into a geospatial impact map.  The transformer architecture captures high-dimensional system states and temporal dependencies, while DRL enables adaptive and scalable decision-making. The framework is trained offline on simulated and historical events and deployed online.  Case studies on the 2869-bus European grid demonstrate improved restoration speed and solution quality.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The integration of transformer architectures with DRL for crew dispatch in post-earthquake grid restoration is a significant strength. Most prior work relies on traditional optimization methods (MILP, heuristics) that struggle with scalability and real-time performance. Transformers are well-suited to capture the temporal dependencies inherent in restoration tasks. The authors' approach to earthquake scenario generation and reduction is well-established, but the *combination* with the DRL framework for operational crew dispatch represents a meaningful contribution. The scenario generation approach is not particularly novel, but its use in conjunction with the DRL is.

*   **Significance:** Improving the speed and efficiency of grid restoration after an earthquake is crucial for mitigating economic and societal losses. The reported reduction in computation time while maintaining high solution quality is potentially very valuable. The focus on a large-scale test case (2869-bus system) enhances the practical relevance of the study. The scalability tests convincingly show the method's suitability to real world cases. The scalability assessment is convincing, although more detailed analysis on the training data set sizes and composition might have strengthened the paper.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Strong results demonstrating substantial speedups compared to traditional methods (CPLEX, Gurobi, GA)
    *   Good scalability, validated on a large-scale power system model
    *   The combination of transformer architecture with DRL is well-motivated.
    *   The method does not require frequent retraining.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The scenario generation and reduction pipeline, while effective, is not particularly novel. More advanced earthquake modeling methods could be explored in future work.
    *   Although the method does not require frequent retraining, the training data and effort required to bootstrap this DRL approach are not entirely clear. The computational resources and time required for training should be quantified.
    *   The evaluation primarily focuses on runtime and objective function value. While important, further analysis on the *quality* of dispatch decisions, e.g., comparing the sequence and selection decisions to known optimal strategies in simplified cases, could strengthen the evaluation.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper provides a promising approach to a critical problem, and the demonstrated performance improvements are compelling. If the method proves robust and generalizable to other grids and disaster scenarios, it could significantly impact how utilities plan for and respond to earthquakes. The learning framework can be used in general resilience management for fast decision support.

*   **Score Rationale:**
    The paper presents a solid advancement in the field of grid resilience by offering a promising DRL-based method for crew dispatch following an earthquake. While the approach to scenario generation is not a breakthrough, the integration of a transformer architecture with DRL for real-time decision-making is both innovative and practically relevant. The authors demonstrate convincing improvements in runtime performance and scalability, strengthening the potential impact of their work. The paper would be significantly strengthened by a more in-depth discussion of training requirements, including the size and composition of the data needed, the sensitivity of the trained model to these datasets, and more insight into specific dispatch decision making in a small simplified case.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: eess.SY
- **Score**: 7/10

### EvoEmo: Towards Evolved Emotional Policies for LLM Agents in Multi-Turn Negotiation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04310v1)
- **Authors**: Yunbo Long, Liming Xu, Lukas Beckenbauer, Yuhan Liu, Alexandra Brintrup
- **Abstract**: Recent research on Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning in Large Language Models (LLMs) has demonstrated that agents can engage in \textit{complex}, \textit{multi-turn} negotiations, opening new avenues for agentic AI. However, existing LLM agents largely overlook the functional role of emotions in such negotiations, instead generating passive, preference-driven emotional responses that make them vulnerable to manipulation and strategic exploitation by adversarial counterparts. To address this gap, we present EvoEmo, an evolutionary reinforcement learning framework that optimizes dynamic emotional expression in negotiations. EvoEmo models emotional state transitions as a Markov Decision Process and employs population-based genetic optimization to evolve high-reward emotion policies across diverse negotiation scenarios. We further propose an evaluation framework with two baselines -- vanilla strategies and fixed-emotion strategies -- for benchmarking emotion-aware negotiation. Extensive experiments and ablation studies show that EvoEmo consistently outperforms both baselines, achieving higher success rates, higher efficiency, and increased buyer savings. This findings highlight the importance of adaptive emotional expression in enabling more effective LLM agents for multi-turn negotiation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "EvoEmo: Towards Evolved Emotional Policies for LLM Agents in Multi-Turn Negotiation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EvoEmo, a novel framework for optimizing emotional policies in LLM agents engaged in multi-turn negotiation scenarios. Recognizing the limitations of existing LLM agents in strategically leveraging emotions, EvoEmo employs evolutionary reinforcement learning to discover optimal emotion transition rules. The framework models emotional state transitions as a Markov Decision Process and uses population-based genetic optimization to evolve high-reward emotion policies.  The authors also introduce an evaluation framework with vanilla and fixed-emotion baselines for benchmarking.  Experiments demonstrate that EvoEmo consistently outperforms both baselines, leading to higher success rates, increased efficiency, and greater buyer savings in negotiations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using evolutionary reinforcement learning to optimize emotion policies for LLM agents in negotiation is relatively novel. While individual components (evolutionary algorithms, reinforcement learning, LLMs in negotiation) are not new, their combination in this specific way to address the strategic use of emotion is a unique contribution. The explicit modeling of emotion transitions and the evaluation framework with specific baselines add to the paper's originality.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in addressing a key gap in current LLM agent research: the lack of strategic emotional intelligence in negotiation.  The findings demonstrate that adaptive emotional expression can significantly enhance the performance of LLM agents, suggesting a promising avenue for developing more sophisticated and effective interactive AI systems. The benchmark and the methodology provide tools for future research in this area. The work highlights that simply recognizing emotions is insufficient; agents need to strategically *use* them.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper provides a clear and well-structured explanation of the EvoEmo framework.
    *   The experimental setup is rigorous, with well-defined baselines and multiple LLM architectures used for evaluation.
    *   The results clearly demonstrate the effectiveness of EvoEmo compared to the baselines.
    *   The paper includes ablation studies to assess the contribution of key design choices.
    *   The authors acknowledge the limitations of their study, providing directions for future research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The emotional spectrum considered (seven basic emotions) is limited and may not fully capture the nuances of human emotional expression.
    *   The negotiation scenarios are confined to conventional commercial contexts, which limits the generalizability of the findings. The results might not hold in high-stakes or highly emotionally charged negotiation settings.
    *   The interpretability of the evolved emotional strategies is limited due to the black-box nature of LLMs and the evolutionary process. Understanding *why* certain strategies work remains challenging. The explanation for different performances across models may be limited.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in LLM agent development, particularly in the areas of negotiation, dialogue systems, and affective computing. It provides a valuable framework and benchmark for developing and evaluating emotion-aware AI systems. The concept of evolving emotional policies could be extended to other interactive tasks beyond negotiation.

**Rigorous Rationale for the Score:**
While the paper makes a novel contribution and demonstrates promising results, its limitations prevent it from achieving a higher score. The restricted emotional spectrum and the limited scope of the negotiation scenarios raise concerns about the generalizability of the findings. Furthermore, the lack of interpretability of the evolved strategies hinders deeper understanding and further development. However, the paper successfully addresses an important problem, offers a robust methodology, and provides compelling evidence for the benefits of adaptive emotional expression in LLM agents, demonstrating a good influence on research in LLM. Therefore:

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Write on Paper, Wrong in Practice: Why LLMs Still Struggle with Writing Clinical Notes
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04340v1)
- **Authors**: Kristina L. Kupferschmidt, Kieran O'Doherty, Joshua A. Skorburg
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are often proposed as tools to streamline clinical documentation, a task viewed as both high-volume and low-risk. However, even seemingly straightforward applications of LLMs raise complex sociotechnical considerations to translate into practice. This case study, conducted at KidsAbility, a pediatric rehabilitation facility in Ontario, Canada examined the use of LLMs to support occupational therapists in reducing documentation burden.We conducted a qualitative study involving 20 clinicians who participated in pilot programs using two AI technologies: a general-purpose proprietary LLM and a bespoke model fine-tuned on proprietary historical documentation. Our findings reveal that documentation challenges are sociotechnical in nature, shaped by clinical workflows, organizational policies, and system constraints. Four key themes emerged: (1) the heterogeneity of workflows, (2) the documentation burden is systemic and not directly linked to the creation of any single type of documentation, (3) the need for flexible tools and clinician autonomy, and (4) effective implementation requires mutual learning between clinicians and AI systems. While LLMs show promise in easing documentation tasks, their success will depend on flexible, adaptive integration that supports clinician autonomy. Beyond technical performance, sustained adoption will require training programs and implementation strategies that reflect the complexity of clinical environments.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Write on Paper, Wrong in Practice: Why LLMs Still Struggle with Writing Clinical Notes":

**Summary:**

This paper examines the challenges of deploying Large Language Models (LLMs) to automate clinical documentation, specifically SOAP notes, in a pediatric rehabilitation center. The study involved a qualitative analysis of interviews with 20 clinicians who participated in pilot programs using both a general-purpose LLM (Microsoft's CoPilot) and a custom-built, fine-tuned LLM based on Llama 3. The researchers found that despite the technical capabilities of LLMs, their real-world utility was hampered by sociotechnical factors, including heterogeneous clinical workflows, systemic documentation burdens, the need for clinician autonomy, and the importance of mutual learning between clinicians and AI systems. The study concludes that successful LLM integration in healthcare requires flexible, adaptive implementation strategies that account for the complexity of clinical environments and support clinician autonomy, rather than simply focusing on technical performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its detailed qualitative exploration of the practical challenges of LLM deployment in a specific healthcare setting, going beyond simple performance metrics. It highlights the "translation gap" between LLM capabilities and real-world clinical utility. While previous research has touched on similar themes (e.g., the importance of workflow integration, clinician buy-in), this paper provides a rich, nuanced case study that illuminates the specific sociotechnical barriers in a well-defined context.
*   **Significance:** The significance of this work is that it provides a cautionary tale about the over-optimistic expectations surrounding LLMs in healthcare. It demonstrates that even seemingly straightforward tasks like SOAP note generation are deeply embedded in complex clinical workflows and organizational policies. By emphasizing the importance of clinician autonomy, flexible implementation, and mutual learning, the paper offers valuable guidance for researchers and practitioners seeking to deploy LLMs in healthcare settings responsibly.
*   **Strengths:** The paper is strong in its methodology. The qualitative approach, using semi-structured interviews, is well-suited to uncover the complex perspectives and experiences of clinicians. The thematic analysis is clearly presented, and the findings are grounded in rich data from the interviews. The use of the FITT framework provides a valuable analytical lens for understanding the interplay of individual, task, and technology factors. The paper is clearly written and well-organized.
*   **Weaknesses:** While the case study approach provides valuable depth, it limits the generalizability of the findings. The study focuses on a single pediatric rehabilitation center in Ontario, Canada. The specific challenges and constraints may not be representative of other healthcare settings or even other rehabilitation centers. It might be improved through comparison of other centres or a broader geographical scope. Also, it provides detail about using SOAP notes but does not analyse its usefulness in general or any other standard documentation system, thereby failing to provide an adequate analysis of other clinical models.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by prompting researchers and practitioners to adopt a more sociotechnical perspective when developing and deploying LLMs in healthcare. It highlights the need to move beyond a purely technical focus and consider the broader organizational, workflow, and human factors that shape adoption and integration. The paper could also inform the design of more effective training programs and implementation strategies for LLMs in clinical settings.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the literature by providing a detailed qualitative case study that exposes the practical challenges of LLM deployment in healthcare. It is well-researched, clearly written, and grounded in a robust theoretical framework. However, the limited generalizability of the case study and the lack of a broader comparative analysis prevent it from achieving a higher score. While it provides valuable insights, further research is needed to confirm the findings in other contexts and to develop more generalizable strategies for LLM integration in healthcare.

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 7/10

### SRWToolkit: An Open Source Wizard of Oz Toolkit to Create Social Robotic Avatars
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04356v1)
- **Authors**: Atikkhan Faridkhan Nilgar, Kristof Van Laerhoven, Ayub Kinoti
- **Abstract**: We present SRWToolkit, an open-source Wizard of Oz toolkit designed to facilitate the rapid prototyping of social robotic avatars powered by local large language models (LLMs). Our web-based toolkit enables multimodal interaction through text input, button-activated speech, and wake-word command. The toolkit offers real-time configuration of avatar appearance, behavior, language, and voice via an intuitive control panel. In contrast to prior works that rely on cloud-based LLM services, SRWToolkit emphasizes modularity and ensures on-device functionality through local LLM inference. In our small-scale user study ($n=11$), participants created and interacted with diverse robotic roles (hospital receptionist, mathematics teacher, and driving assistant), which demonstrated positive outcomes in the toolkit's usability, trust, and user experience. The toolkit enables rapid and efficient development of robot characters customized to researchers' needs, supporting scalable research in human-robot interaction.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SRWToolkit, an open-source Wizard of Oz (WoZ) toolkit designed to streamline the prototyping of social robotic avatars.  The toolkit uses local Large Language Models (LLMs) to power interactions, prioritizing on-device functionality and modularity.  SRWToolkit offers features such as multimodal interaction (text input, button-activated speech, wake-word commands), real-time avatar configuration, and an intuitive control panel.  A small user study involving the creation and interaction with various robotic roles (hospital receptionist, math teacher, driving assistant) showed positive results in usability, trust, and user experience.  The authors emphasize the toolkit's ability to enable rapid and efficient development of robot characters, supporting scalable research in Human-Robot Interaction (HRI).

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** While the concept of using WoZ techniques and LLMs for HRI prototyping isn't entirely new (as acknowledged by the authors in the related works section), SRWToolkit distinguishes itself through its focus on *local LLM inference* and its *modular design*, offering an open-source solution emphasizing data privacy and reducing latency. The combination of these aspects, along with a user-friendly interface and multimodal interaction capabilities, contributes to its novelty. The use of local LLMs is a significant step forward, especially addressing privacy concerns which have been a major roadblock to broader adoption of LLM powered conversational agents.

*   **Significance:** The toolkit addresses a crucial bottleneck in social robotics research: the complexity and multidisciplinary nature of building interactive robot systems. By reducing the technical barrier to entry, SRWToolkit has the potential to broaden participation in HRI research, enabling researchers with varying levels of technical expertise to rapidly prototype and experiment with different interaction scenarios. The modular design and the focus on on-device LLMs make it an appealing alternative to cloud-based solutions. The usability and positive user experience reported in the user study suggest that the toolkit is effective in achieving its goals. The user study's roles are also well-selected, showing application across different HRI domains.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Open-source and accessible: Promotes wider adoption and community contributions.
    *   Local LLM inference: Addresses privacy concerns and latency issues.
    *   Multimodal interaction: Provides a richer and more natural interaction experience.
    *   User-friendly interface: Simplifies avatar configuration and control.
    *   Modular design: Enhances flexibility and extensibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Small-scale user study: Limited generalizability of the findings. The sample size (n=11) is relatively small, and the participants were primarily university students and colleagues with some familiarity with chatbot technologies.
    *   Dependency on Google Cloud APIs for speech processing (currently): This limits its full offline functionality and introduces a reliance on external services, contradicting the focus on local LLM inference.
    *   Screen-based avatar: Lack of physical embodiment limits the scope of HRI research that can be conducted.
    *   Limited exploration of the impact of specific role types and avatar characteristics. The paper mentions plans for future research to address this.
    *   Does not provide a comparative study to other frameworks like WebWOZ or WoZ4U. While the paper describes the advantages of the framework, there is no performance or feature comparison with any related works.

* **Impact:** The toolkit will enable researchers to investigate human-robot interactions that were previously difficult or impractical to explore. The focus on local LLMs opens possibilities for deploying conversational robots in privacy-sensitive contexts (e.g., healthcare). The toolkit's design may inspire the development of other similar tools for different domains.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**

SRWToolkit makes a significant contribution to the field by providing a practical and accessible open-source toolkit for rapid prototyping of social robotic avatars with local LLM support. Its novel focus on on-device LLMs and modular design addresses important limitations of existing solutions. While the user study is limited in scale and the reliance on Google Cloud APIs represents a drawback, the toolkit's strengths outweigh its weaknesses, making it a valuable resource for the HRI research community. A higher score is reserved for works with more robust validation and a deeper exploration of the design space. While the work has promise, more extensive evaluation is needed. The choice of an open-source approach is a positive one and will facilitate community refinement of the toolkit over time.

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 7/10

### Connections between reinforcement learning with feedback,test-time scaling, and diffusion guidance: An anthology
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04372v1)
- **Authors**: Yuchen Jiao, Yuxin Chen, Gen Li
- **Abstract**: In this note, we reflect on several fundamental connections among widely used post-training techniques. We clarify some intimate connections and equivalences between reinforcement learning with human feedback, reinforcement learning with internal feedback, and test-time scaling (particularly soft best-of-$N$ sampling), while also illuminating intrinsic links between diffusion guidance and test-time scaling. Additionally, we introduce a resampling approach for alignment and reward-directed diffusion models, sidestepping the need for explicit reinforcement learning techniques.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores connections between several popular post-training techniques for language and diffusion models: Reinforcement Learning with Human Feedback (RLHF), Reinforcement Learning with Internal Feedback (RLIF), test-time scaling (particularly best-of-N sampling), and diffusion guidance. The authors establish equivalences and relationships between these methods under specific conditions. Key contributions include showing that RLHF and RLIF can be formulated equivalently under suitable conditions, demonstrating the asymptotic equivalence of soft best-of-N sampling and RLHF/RLIF, and proposing an "RL-free alignment" approach that avoids explicit reinforcement learning through importance sampling. They also extend best-of-N sampling to diffusion models for classifier-free guidance and introduce an RL-free method for reward-directed diffusion models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's core strength lies in identifying and formalizing connections between seemingly disparate post-training techniques. While individual techniques like RLHF, RLIF, and best-of-N sampling are well-established, explicitly linking them and demonstrating their equivalences adds a valuable layer of understanding. The proposal of an "RL-free alignment" approach and its application to diffusion models represents a novel contribution, though its practical effectiveness remains to be thoroughly evaluated. While the observation that RLHF is just an exponential tilt is not new (already present in prior work like Ziegler et al., 2019), presenting all connections together from an anthology provides a comprehensive summary that is helpful to the field.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper stems from its potential to:
    *   **Unify understanding:** By clarifying the connections between different techniques, the paper could facilitate a more holistic understanding of post-training alignment.
    *   **Inform algorithm design:** The identified equivalences could inspire new algorithm designs, potentially leading to more efficient and effective alignment methods.
    *   **Simplify implementation:** The "RL-free alignment" approach, if successful, offers a potentially simpler and more direct alternative to complex RL pipelines, especially for diffusion models where score functions are often easier to manipulate than full policies.
    *   **Improved diffusion models:** Providing alternative algorithms for reward-directed diffusion models helps to improve the sample quality and overall performance of these models, which are used in a variety of creative tasks.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear and concise presentation:** The paper is well-written and presents the core ideas in a relatively accessible manner, given the technical nature of the subject matter.
    *   **Mathematical rigor:** The paper provides mathematical derivations to support its claims, which adds credibility to its findings.
    *   **Comprehensive scope:** The paper covers a range of post-training techniques, contributing to a more unified understanding of the field.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Theoretical focus:** The paper is primarily theoretical, and the practical implications of the proposed "RL-free alignment" approach need to be validated through empirical evaluation on real-world datasets.
    *   **Limited scope:** The identified equivalences hold under specific conditions, which may not always be met in practice. The paper does not fully explore the limitations of these equivalences.
    *   **Assumptions:** The findings are often based on the assumption of an accurate reward model (r_theta = r) or a reference policy that is aligned with human values, which may not always be true in practice. This limits the real-world application of the theoretical conclusions.

**Overall Assessment:**
The paper makes a solid contribution to the field by highlighting connections between post-training techniques and introducing a novel, though as-yet-untested, alternative to RL-based alignment.

Score: 7

**Rationale:**
The score reflects the paper's theoretical contributions and potential for impact. The paper provides insightful connections among the covered techniques and proposes a novel RL-free alignment approach, which merits a relatively high score. However, the score is not higher because the paper is mainly theoretical and lacks empirical validation. The significance of the paper is limited by the assumptions used for achieving the theoretical connections. Empirical evidence is needed to demonstrate the practical effectiveness of the "RL-free alignment" and to explore the robustness of the findings under varying conditions. Further, the reliance on strong assumptions, such as perfect reward models and reference policies aligned with human values, diminishes the practical relevance of the theoretical results.

- **Classification**: stat.ML
- **Score**: 7/10

### Aesthetic Image Captioning with Saliency Enhanced MLLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04378v1)
- **Authors**: Yilin Tao, Jiashui Huang, Huaze Xu, Ling Shao
- **Abstract**: Aesthetic Image Captioning (AIC) aims to generate textual descriptions of image aesthetics, becoming a key research direction in the field of computational aesthetics. In recent years, pretrained Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have advanced rapidly, leading to a significant increase in image aesthetics research that integrates both visual and textual modalities. However, most existing studies on image aesthetics primarily focus on predicting aesthetic ratings and have shown limited application in AIC. Existing AIC works leveraging MLLMs predominantly rely on fine-tuning methods without specifically adapting MLLMs to focus on target aesthetic content. To address this limitation, we propose the Aesthetic Saliency Enhanced Multimodal Large Language Model (ASE-MLLM), an end-to-end framework that explicitly incorporates aesthetic saliency into MLLMs. Within this framework, we introduce the Image Aesthetic Saliency Module (IASM), which efficiently and effectively extracts aesthetic saliency features from images. Additionally, we design IAS-ViT as the image encoder for MLLMs, this module fuses aesthetic saliency features with original image features via a cross-attention mechanism. To the best of our knowledge, ASE-MLLM is the first framework to integrate image aesthetic saliency into MLLMs specifically for AIC tasks. Extensive experiments demonstrated that our approach significantly outperformed traditional methods and generic MLLMs on current mainstream AIC benchmarks, achieving state-of-the-art (SOTA) performance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary**

The paper introduces a new framework called Aesthetic Saliency Enhanced Multimodal Large Language Model (ASE-MLLM) for aesthetic image captioning (AIC).  The core idea is to explicitly incorporate aesthetic saliency into MLLMs to improve their ability to generate descriptions that focus on the aesthetic qualities of an image. The framework includes:

1.  **Image Aesthetic Saliency Module (IASM):**  This module extracts aesthetic saliency features from images, focusing on regions that contribute significantly to aesthetic appeal.
2.  **IAS-ViT Encoder:**  A modified Vision Transformer that fuses aesthetic saliency features with original image features using a cross-attention mechanism, guiding the model to focus on high-aesthetic-value regions.

The authors claim that ASE-MLLM outperforms traditional methods and generic MLLMs on AIC benchmarks, achieving state-of-the-art results.  Experiments are performed using three common AIC datasets.

**Critical Evaluation**

**Novelty:**

The core novelty of the paper lies in the explicit integration of *aesthetic saliency* into an MLLM-based AIC framework.  Existing AIC works predominantly fine-tune MLLMs without explicitly guiding them towards regions of high aesthetic importance. The introduction of IASM and its integration with a ViT via cross-attention presents a novel approach to focus MLLMs on relevant aesthetic details. While existing works have touched upon visual saliency for aesthetic tasks, differentiating and leveraging *aesthetic* saliency, which goes beyond foreground object detection and encapsulates compositional and contextual elements, is a key contribution.

**Significance:**

The significance stems from addressing a core limitation of current MLLM-based AIC systems: their inability to effectively discern and focus on aesthetic elements. By incorporating aesthetic saliency, the model is likely to generate more relevant and nuanced captions that better capture the subjective qualities of an image.  The state-of-the-art performance reported on benchmark datasets provides empirical evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed approach. If the gains are consistent across datasets and tasks, this work could pave the way for more sophisticated and aesthetically aware image understanding systems. However, it is important to ensure if improvements come from more general dataset shifts instead of an actual improvement of the aesthetic qualities in the generated caption. Furthermore, a human evaluation of generated captions could lend more support to the claims in the paper.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing AIC approaches and motivates the need for aesthetic saliency.
*   **Well-Defined Framework:**  The ASE-MLLM framework is well-structured and includes a dedicated module (IASM) for extracting aesthetic saliency features.
*   **Technical Soundness:**  The use of cross-attention for fusing saliency and image features is a technically sound approach.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The authors present experimental results demonstrating the superiority of their approach over baseline methods.
*   **Generalizability**: The authors demonstrate that the framework can be generalized to different underlying base MLLMs.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Analysis of Aesthetic Saliency:** The paper doesn't provide extensive qualitative examples or a deeper analysis of the types of aesthetic saliency captured by the IASM.  A visualization of the saliency maps and a discussion of the features being extracted would strengthen the paper.
*   **Subjectivity of Aesthetics:** The definition and quantification of aesthetics are inherently subjective. While the paper leverages existing datasets and metrics, it's important to acknowledge the limitations of these proxies.
*   **Metric Dependency:** The reported results rely heavily on automatic evaluation metrics. While these metrics are commonly used, they may not fully capture the quality and nuance of aesthetic descriptions. A human evaluation of generated captions would provide a more robust assessment.
*   **Lack of ablation over the IASM**: The paper presents an ablation over including IASM, but does not analyze how its different components contribute to performance.
*   **Limited novelty in IAS-ViT**: The paper uses a standard ViT architecture with a cross-attention layer, and the novelty of that component is small. The adaptation to AIC is the main novelty.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence future research in AIC by:

*   **Highlighting the Importance of Aesthetic Saliency:** The work emphasizes the need to explicitly model aesthetic qualities rather than relying solely on generic image features.
*   **Providing a Concrete Framework:** The ASE-MLLM framework provides a blueprint for incorporating aesthetic saliency into MLLMs.
*   **Inspiring New Saliency Extraction Techniques:** The IASM module could inspire further research into developing more sophisticated techniques for extracting aesthetic saliency features.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and technically sound approach to AIC by explicitly integrating aesthetic saliency into MLLMs. The empirical results demonstrate a significant improvement over existing methods and its framework generalizes well to different base MLLMs. However, the novelty of the cross-attention module is modest, the evaluation relies heavily on automatic metrics, the analysis of aesthetic saliency is limited, and there's a lack of ablation experiments for the IASM architecture itself. The potential impact on the field is significant, but further research is needed to address the limitations and explore the full potential of aesthetic saliency in AIC. Therefore, I assign a score of 7.5, acknowledging its significant contribution while also highlighting areas for improvement.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### SSGaussian: Semantic-Aware and Structure-Preserving 3D Style Transfer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04379v1)
- **Authors**: Jimin Xu, Bosheng Qin, Tao Jin, Zhou Zhao, Zhenhui Ye, Jun Yu, Fei Wu
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in neural representations, such as Neural Radiance Fields and 3D Gaussian Splatting, have increased interest in applying style transfer to 3D scenes. While existing methods can transfer style patterns onto 3D-consistent neural representations, they struggle to effectively extract and transfer high-level style semantics from the reference style image. Additionally, the stylized results often lack structural clarity and separation, making it difficult to distinguish between different instances or objects within the 3D scene. To address these limitations, we propose a novel 3D style transfer pipeline that effectively integrates prior knowledge from pretrained 2D diffusion models. Our pipeline consists of two key stages: First, we leverage diffusion priors to generate stylized renderings of key viewpoints. Then, we transfer the stylized key views onto the 3D representation. This process incorporates two innovative designs. The first is cross-view style alignment, which inserts cross-view attention into the last upsampling block of the UNet, allowing feature interactions across multiple key views. This ensures that the diffusion model generates stylized key views that maintain both style fidelity and instance-level consistency. The second is instance-level style transfer, which effectively leverages instance-level consistency across stylized key views and transfers it onto the 3D representation. This results in a more structured, visually coherent, and artistically enriched stylization. Extensive qualitative and quantitative experiments demonstrate that our 3D style transfer pipeline significantly outperforms state-of-the-art methods across a wide range of scenes, from forward-facing to challenging 360-degree environments. Visit our project page https://jm-xu.github.io/SSGaussian for immersive visualization.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "SSGaussian: Semantic-Aware and Structure-Preserving 3D Style Transfer":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel pipeline, SSGaussian, for 3D style transfer that leverages 2D diffusion priors to achieve semantic-aware and structure-preserving stylization of 3D scenes. The pipeline consists of two main stages: (1) Consistent multi-view stylization of key viewpoints using a pretrained diffusion model enhanced with a Cross-View Style Alignment (CVSA) module and (2) 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) stylization achieved via an Instance-level Style Transfer (IST) approach.  The CVSA module enforces instance-level consistency across different viewpoints during diffusion model inference, and the IST approach uses group matching to transfer style information from the stylized key views to the 3DGS representation. The authors demonstrate that their approach outperforms existing methods in both qualitative and quantitative evaluations, achieving more structured, visually coherent, and artistically enriched stylization results across various scenes.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's key strength lies in its effective integration of 2D diffusion priors into a 3D style transfer pipeline. The Cross-View Style Alignment (CVSA) module is a particularly innovative contribution, addressing a crucial challenge in multi-view consistent stylization by focusing on instance-level coherence rather than attempting pixel-perfect alignment. The Instance-level Style Transfer (IST) with group matching is also a clever way to leverage the diffusion priors in the 3D domain, improving the structure and visual coherence of the final stylized scene.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant limitation in existing 3D style transfer methods, which often struggle to effectively extract and transfer high-level style semantics from reference images and often produce results that lack structural clarity. SSGaussian offers a solution to these problems, leading to visually improved results.

*   **Experimental Evaluation:** The paper provides a comprehensive experimental evaluation, including both qualitative and quantitative comparisons with state-of-the-art methods. The ablation studies clearly demonstrate the effectiveness of the CVSA and IST modules. The user study adds further weight to the claims of improved stylization quality.  The method is evaluated on two different datasets (LLFF and Tanks & Temples).

*   **Clarity:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed method and its components. The illustrations and figures are helpful in understanding the pipeline and the results.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Reliance on Pre-trained Models:** The method relies on pre-trained diffusion models and 3DGS representations. While this is common in the field, it means that the performance of the method is tied to the capabilities of these underlying models. Any limitations or biases in these models may be propagated to the stylized results.

*   **Computational Cost:** While the paper claims real-time rendering speeds, it also mentions a training time of 20 minutes which is not inexpensive. A more thorough analysis of the computational cost, including memory requirements and the impact of different parameters on performance, would be beneficial. The method builds upon an already computationally expensive 3DGS framework, potentially limiting its widespread adoption.

*   **Generalizability:** While the method is evaluated on a variety of scenes, it is unclear how well it would generalize to scenes with significantly different characteristics (e.g., scenes with complex lighting or dynamic objects). The results shown are compelling, but a more diverse set of style exemplars and target scenes could further strengthen the claims of generalizability.

*   **Metrics:** The quantitative metrics focus primarily on consistency and perceptual metrics.  While these are important, they don't fully capture the subjective aspects of style transfer quality. The user study partially addresses this, but a more comprehensive set of evaluation metrics might be desirable.

**Overall Assessment:**

SSGaussian represents a significant advancement in the field of 3D style transfer. The clever integration of 2D diffusion priors, coupled with the innovative CVSA and IST modules, leads to demonstrably improved stylization results. The paper is well-written, thoroughly evaluated, and addresses a clear limitation in existing methods. While the reliance on pre-trained models and computational cost are valid concerns, the overall contribution of the paper is substantial. The introduction of instance-level consistency and stylization opens new avenues for future research in this area.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and effective approach to 3D style transfer, addressing a significant challenge in the field. The experimental results are compelling, and the ablation studies provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed modules.  The work is not a complete paradigm shift but rather a well-engineered advancement that significantly improves the quality and coherence of 3D style transfer.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Denoising GER: A Noise-Robust Generative Error Correction with LLM for Speech Recognition
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04392v1)
- **Authors**: Yanyan Liu, Minqiang Xu, Yihao Chen, Liang He, Lei Fang, Sian Fang, Lin Liu
- **Abstract**: In recent years, large language models (LLM) have made significant progress in the task of generation error correction (GER) for automatic speech recognition (ASR) post-processing. However, in complex noisy environments, they still face challenges such as poor adaptability and low information utilization, resulting in limited effectiveness of GER. To address these issues, this paper proposes a noise-robust multi-modal GER framework (Denoising GER). The framework enhances the model's adaptability to different noisy scenarios through a noise-adaptive acoustic encoder and optimizes the integration of multi-modal information via a heterogeneous feature compensation dynamic fusion (HFCDF) mechanism, improving the LLM's utilization of multi-modal information. Additionally, reinforcement learning (RL) training strategies are introduced to enhance the model's predictive capabilities. Experimental results demonstrate that Denoising GER significantly improves accuracy and robustness in noisy environments and exhibits good generalization abilities in unseen noise scenarios.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Denoising GER: A Noise-Robust Generative Error Correction with LLM for Speech Recognition" addresses the problem of automatic speech recognition (ASR) performance degradation in noisy environments. It proposes a framework called Denoising GER, which integrates a noise-adaptive acoustic encoder (NAAE), a heterogeneous feature compensation dynamic fusion (HFCDF) mechanism, and a reinforcement learning-based training strategy (RL-loss) to improve the robustness of LLM-based generative error correction (GER). The NAAE is designed to extract high-quality speech embeddings even in noisy conditions. The HFCDF aims to improve the integration of multi-modal information (speech and text) by dynamically adjusting fusion weights. RL-loss is used to optimize the training objective using ASR evaluation metrics directly. The authors demonstrate through experiments on synthetic and real noisy speech datasets that their approach significantly improves accuracy and robustness in noisy environments compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a comprehensive framework with multiple novel components that tackles a practical and important problem: noisy ASR. The integration of NAAE, HFCDF, and RL-loss is a significant contribution. The NAAE is a good way to help the acoustic encoder handle noise. The HFCDF is a good way to fuse information for speech and text data. The RL-loss training objective that correlates to ASR metrics are also useful.
    *   **Significance:** ASR in noisy environments is a well-known challenge with important implications for real-world applications.  The proposed framework offers a potential solution with demonstrated improvements over previous approaches.
    *   **Completeness:** The experimental setup is thorough and includes both synthetic and real-world datasets.  Ablation studies are included to demonstrate the value of each component of the framework. Error case analysis provides further insights into the model's behaviour.
    *   **Clarity:** The paper is generally well-written and organized. The explanation of the different components and the overall architecture is clear.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Technical Depth:** Although the components are well-explained, the novelty of each individual part relative to other work in acoustic modeling, multi-modal fusion, and RL training needs further clarification. The RL-loss training can be considered an integration method with existing training and methods.
    *   **Practical Considerations:** The computational requirements of the framework (using LLMs and fine-tuning modules) might limit its adoption in resource-constrained environments. Addressing efficiency might be a valuable direction for future work. The transfer to unseen noise condition is a strong point, but generalization with real word dataset is more important.
    *   **Justification of Choices:** While the paper details the approach taken, deeper justifications for specific architectural choices (e.g., the U-Net adapter within the NAAE) would strengthen the argument.

*   **Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a significant contribution to the field of noisy ASR by proposing a comprehensive and well-evaluated framework, Denoising GER. While the individual components might not be entirely groundbreaking, their integration and the demonstrated improvements in robustness and accuracy are compelling.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.SD
- **Score**: 7/10

### Transition Models: Rethinking the Generative Learning Objective
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04394v1)
- **Authors**: Zidong Wang, Yiyuan Zhang, Xiaoyu Yue, Xiangyu Yue, Yangguang Li, Wanli Ouyang, Lei Bai
- **Abstract**: A fundamental dilemma in generative modeling persists: iterative diffusion models achieve outstanding fidelity, but at a significant computational cost, while efficient few-step alternatives are constrained by a hard quality ceiling. This conflict between generation steps and output quality arises from restrictive training objectives that focus exclusively on either infinitesimal dynamics (PF-ODEs) or direct endpoint prediction. We address this challenge by introducing an exact, continuous-time dynamics equation that analytically defines state transitions across any finite time interval. This leads to a novel generative paradigm, Transition Models (TiM), which adapt to arbitrary-step transitions, seamlessly traversing the generative trajectory from single leaps to fine-grained refinement with more steps. Despite having only 865M parameters, TiM achieves state-of-the-art performance, surpassing leading models such as SD3.5 (8B parameters) and FLUX.1 (12B parameters) across all evaluated step counts. Importantly, unlike previous few-step generators, TiM demonstrates monotonic quality improvement as the sampling budget increases. Additionally, when employing our native-resolution strategy, TiM delivers exceptional fidelity at resolutions up to 4096x4096.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper introduces Transition Models (TiM), a novel generative modeling approach designed to overcome the trade-off between fidelity and computational efficiency in existing generative models, particularly diffusion models. TiM learns state transitions over arbitrary time intervals along the Probability Flow Ordinary Differential Equation (PF-ODE) trajectory. This allows it to adapt to different step sizes during the sampling process, combining the strengths of few-step generators and multi-step refinement approaches. The authors demonstrate TiM's performance on text-to-image and class-conditional image generation tasks. A TiM model with only 865M parameters achieves state-of-the-art results on the GenEval benchmark, surpassing larger models like SD3.5 and FLUX.1, and demonstrates monotonic quality improvement with increasing function evaluations (NFEs). It also delivers exceptional fidelity at high resolutions (up to 4096x4096).  A key element is the Differential Derivation Equation (DDE) which enables a scalable and from-scratch training.

**Critical Evaluation**

*Novelty:*
The core novelty lies in the formulation of the training objective, which is based on learning transitions between states at arbitrary time intervals (∆t) rather than focusing solely on infinitesimal dynamics (like PF-ODEs) or direct endpoint prediction (like consistency models). The derivation of the State Transition Identity (a product-derivative invariant) provides a principled way to achieve both trajectory consistency and a smoother solution manifold.  The introduction of the Differential Derivation Equation (DDE) is a significant practical contribution enabling scalable from-scratch training of TiM compared to existing techniques like JVP which are not compatible with FSDP and FlashAttention.

*Significance:*
The significance stems from addressing a fundamental limitation in generative modeling – the fidelity/efficiency trade-off. By creating a model that can be used both as a powerful few-step generator *and* a refinable integrator, TiM potentially opens up new possibilities for generative applications where computational resources are limited or where iterative refinement is desired. The empirical results, particularly the state-of-the-art performance on GenEval with a relatively small model, are compelling. The demonstration of monotonic quality improvement with increasing steps is a valuable attribute, distinguishing it from many few-step methods that saturate quickly. The scalability of the approach with the DDE will certainly impact the generative model field by enabling more efficient large scale training, and potentially leading to larger and better performing models trained from scratch. The native-resolution training also contributes to the ability to scale to very high-resolution image generation.

*Strengths:*
*   Strong theoretical foundation with the derivation of the State Transition Identity.
*   Practical solution for scalable training (DDE).
*   State-of-the-art empirical results.
*   Monotonic quality improvement with increasing steps.
*   Ability to generate high-resolution images.
*   From-scratch training.

*Weaknesses:*
*   The paper could benefit from a more extensive discussion of the limitations. Though the authors mention challenges related to content safety, controllability, and fine-grained detail, further exploration of these areas would strengthen the analysis.
*   While the paper mentions the use of LoRA-AdaLN, a more detailed description of its implementation and impact on performance would be valuable.
*   While performance on GenEval benchmark is good, other metrics like human evaluations are missing for text-to-image generation to confirm that the results will generalize.

*Potential Influence:*
TiM has the potential to influence future research in several ways:
*   It encourages a rethinking of generative learning objectives beyond infinitesimal dynamics and endpoint prediction.
*   It provides a practical framework for building versatile generative models that can adapt to different computational budgets.
*   The scalability improvements (DDE) can lead to more efficient training of foundation models.
*   The results can help influence the research on both few-step generative models as well as high-resolution image synthesis.

*Score Justification:*

I assign this paper a score of **8**. While it doesn't revolutionize the field of generative modeling, TiM offers a significant and well-supported improvement over existing approaches. The combination of a sound theoretical framework, a practical scalability solution, and compelling empirical results makes it a noteworthy contribution. The monotonic quality improvement and high-resolution generation capabilities are particularly valuable aspects that could drive further research. The scalable from-scratch training is a welcome contribution in an area where pre-training and complex training pipelines are common. However, the limitations in the areas of fine-grained detail, human evaluation, content control, and model safety slightly hold the paper back from being a truly exceptional contribution.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Self-adaptive Dataset Construction for Real-World Multimodal Safety Scenarios
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04403v1)
- **Authors**: Jingen Qu, Lijun Li, Bo Zhang, Yichen Yan, Jing Shao
- **Abstract**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) are rapidly evolving, presenting increasingly complex safety challenges. However, current dataset construction methods, which are risk-oriented, fail to cover the growing complexity of real-world multimodal safety scenarios (RMS). And due to the lack of a unified evaluation metric, their overall effectiveness remains unproven. This paper introduces a novel image-oriented self-adaptive dataset construction method for RMS, which starts with images and end constructing paired text and guidance responses. Using the image-oriented method, we automatically generate an RMS dataset comprising 35k image-text pairs with guidance responses. Additionally, we introduce a standardized safety dataset evaluation metric: fine-tuning a safety judge model and evaluating its capabilities on other safety datasets.Extensive experiments on various tasks demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed image-oriented pipeline. The results confirm the scalability and effectiveness of the image-oriented approach, offering a new perspective for the construction of real-world multimodal safety datasets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel image-oriented self-adaptive method for constructing real-world multimodal safety scenario (RMS) datasets. The method aims to address the limitations of current risk-oriented datasets, which often rely on synthetic images and predefined risks, making them less sensitive to the complexities of real-world scenarios. The proposed approach starts with real-world images and then generates paired text and guidance responses based on information complementarity—situations where individually safe modalities combine to create unsafe outcomes.  The paper presents a pipeline for automating the RMS dataset construction and categorizes fine-grained scenarios. Furthermore, the paper introduces a standardized evaluation metric based on fine-tuning a safety judge model to evaluate safety datasets effectively.  The authors construct a 35k image-text pair RMS dataset and conduct experiments demonstrating the effectiveness of the approach, showcasing its scalability and potential for improving MLLM safety judgment.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The image-oriented dataset construction is the core novelty. Shifting the focus from risk-oriented generation to identifying potential safety risks from real-world images provides a new perspective on dataset creation. The introduction of information complementarity for identifying risks and using MLLMs for automatic annotation is also innovative. Furthermore, the introduction of a standardized evaluation for safety datasets by fine-tuning a safety judge is novel.
